# Known issues

Failing or flaky tests and deferred items, in one place. `STATUS.md` says where the plan is; this file says what is
known to be wrong or unfinished. Remove an entry when it is fixed (the commit that fixes it removes it), and add one
whenever a day leaves something broken, unmeasured or deferred. Entries state facts that were observed, with where. A flaky or failing test names the phase it belongs to (its `phaseN` label), because a scoped run (`scripts/test-phase.sh`) of another phase does not run it.

## Flaky tests

- 2026-11-19 (Windows PC, phases 23 and 24, `build-clang-cl` plain, `-j 16` scoped run): `engine_c_acceptance_scene`, `engine_c_acceptance_scene_vulkan` and `engine_c_runtime` (a timeout) failed together in one loaded run, and passed alone right after (all three, and again in the next `engine_library` run).

- 2026-11-19 (Windows PC, phase 7, plain, `build-msvc`, crash tests run with `ctest -L crash -j 16 --repeat until-fail`): a death-test child that installs the crash handler hung until the 60 s deadline with nothing on standard error, once in each of two loaded runs: the child of `Windows: install, uninstall, install and a refused second install each call the earlier filter once` (`dt_e2e_win_host_filter_reinstall`, round 4 of 4) and the child of `Windows: while the crash handler is installed, abort() shows no dialog and calls no error reporting` (`dt_e2e_win_abort_flags`, which only installs, uninstalls and exits). Thirty runs of the first alone passed. The reinstall children now start `hang_watchdog` (30 s), so the next hang prints its stacks. After the rebase onto Day 1, the asan run (`-L asan -j 16`) failed `Windows: while the crash handler is installed, abort() shows no dialog and calls no error reporting [asan]` once (output not kept), and it passed 5 of 5 alone. In the same day's full plain run (`-j 16`), `Windows: the earlier filter's answer does not keep the process alive, and a fault inside it does not loop` failed once (no output kept) and passed 5 of 5 alone.
- 2026-11-19 (Windows PC, phase 22, plain, `build-msvc`, full run `-j 16`): `async: a load by path waits for the table, and fails with NotFound once every table is in` failed once (output not kept); 5 of 5 alone, and 5 of 5 of its `[asan]` twin.
- 2026-11-19 (Windows PC, phase 13, plain, `build-msvc`): `Windows: the mouse fills position, buttons with capture, and the wheel in the Linux units` failed in the full run and 1 in 5 repeated (`win32_input_windows_test.cpp:400`, `w.input.pointer_inside == (WindowFromPoint(cursor) == w.Hwnd())`, `false == true`); the test compares with the real cursor's position.

## Unverified by a human

- **Log devices (maintenance, 2026-11-20, Linux; `plans/maintenance/log-devices.md`): open.** (1) The PC (2026-11-20): the four Windows trees build clean, and the plain `build-msvc` foundation, io, settings, windows, gate and docs tests pass (325, after the fixes in the log everything entry); asan and clang-cl not run there. Not built or run on the Mac: `os/terminal.*` (the Windows half in `os/windows/os.cpp`: `IsTerminal`, `EnableTerminalSequences`), `io/file_log_device.*`, `log/tty_log_device.*`, `tests/foundation/{log_device,terminal}_test.cpp`, `tests/io/file_log_device_test.cpp`, the settings test. (2) Windows enables `ENABLE_VIRTUAL_TERMINAL_PROCESSING` on the console and never restores the mode when the program ends. (3) The `Auto` console mode's choice of the coloured device on a real terminal has no test (tests replace the write sink, which forces the plain device) and there is no pty test of `IsTerminal`. (4) `FileLogDevice` writes the line as formatted, with no timestamp. (5) The stack-use rule: `TtyLogDevice::Write` and `StdoutLogDevice::Write` keep up to 5 KB on the stack. (6) The review's regression tests for the oversized TTY record and the trace cap were not confirmed to fail without the fixes.
- **Log everything (maintenance, 2026-11-20, Linux; `plans/maintenance/log-everything.md`): open.** (1) Not built or run on the Mac: the migrated `game/`, `tools/`, `tests/` files and `out::` itself. The PC (2026-11-20): two Windows-only files did not build (`tools/crash_extract/windows/resolve.cpp` had no `out` alias; `windows_manifest_probe` did not link `foundation_base`) and are fixed; `out::Print` writes `\n` as is where the old path wrote `\r\n` (through the C runtime), so the docgen child-process tests now expect `\n`; plain `build-msvc` over the labels foundation, io, settings, windows, boundary and docs: 325 of 325 after these fixes (the first run: 4 failed, the three child-process tests and a stale `docs/reference/symbols.txt`, regenerated). Not run on the PC: asan, clang-cl and the other labels (renderer, ui, asset, physics, script, audio and the demos). (2) Only `script_demo`'s diagnostics moved to `Log`; the other `out::PrintError` calls are bare stderr output without channel or level. (3) Every `out::Print` formats into a 16 KiB stack buffer and writes unbuffered (one write per call). (4) `log.console=off` silences program output as well as log lines. (5) The rhi/Vulkan tests failed in the Linux closing runs where no GPU was found (`vulkaninfo` finds none); not caused by this series. (6) The regression test for `PrintLine` was not confirmed to fail without the fix.
- **C API script physics (maintenance, 2026-11-20, Linux; `plans/maintenance/c-api-script-physics.md`): the PC and the Mac are open.** The PC (2026-11-20): `build-msvc` and `build-clang-cl` build clean, and `engine_c_physics_sample`, `engine_c_script_character` (plain and asan MSVC, plain clang-cl) and the physics tests pass; the `script_physics` tests and the unity-off and shared trees not run. Not built or run on macOS: the character set moved to physics (`engine/physics/character_set.*`, its snapshot), the restore keeping the pre-step hook (`physics/snapshot.cpp`), `character.set_position` and the placeholder `character.create` (`script_physics/physics_bindings.*`), the C API's one set, held move, interpolated write-back, component sync and snapshot footer (`engine_c/runtime.*`), the physics bindings, layers setting and events in C API worlds (`engine_c/script_api.*`, `engine_c/CMakeLists.txt`), and the tests (`tests/engine_library/host_script_character.c` and its CMake and `tests/phases.txt` lines, `physics_sample.c`, `tests/physics/snapshot_test.cpp`, `tests/script_physics/script_physics_events_test.cpp`). `physics_sample`'s golden checksum is now `661cbfc4` (it asserts it on every machine). Not tested: a library built without the script module (every Linux build has it; the stub `ScriptPhysicsEvents` is unexercised). Limits seen on Linux: the handle `entity.spawn` returns names the new entity only within that tick's merge, so a stored handle does not reach it in later ticks (the spawning callback must set the entity up); and a character pressed against dynamic boxes it pushes reports `on_ground` 0 while its height stays put and its vertical speed keeps growing (`physics_sample`: from frame ~204 at the box stack, -16.5 m/s at frame 305, y 0.92), the same at `3be0d921` before this day, so it drops fast when the box clears. Seen once (plain `--affected` run of that day, while a GCC build ran beside it): `X11: text set on one connection is read on another, for every kind of text` (phase 15) failed; it passed alone; it failed once more in the plain `--affected` run of Phase 28 Day 495 (passes alone).

- **Live spatial voices (maintenance, 2026-11-18, Linux; `plans/maintenance/host-live-spatial-voices.md`):** built and run on Linux only (plain, asan, tsan: `engine_c_audio_live`, the `Push stream` unit tests). The delay figure (at most 256 frames, 5.33 ms from a push to the output, `prefill_frames` 0, 480-frame calls) is Linux's; the Windows PC and the Mac have not measured it; the Mac ran the tests on 2026-11-19 (`engine_c_audio_live` and the five `Push stream` unit tests, plain only, passing; also against the shared library). Recorded limits: a push stream has the mixer's sample rate and is not resampled; one thread pushes to a stream at a time (a second concurrent call is refused and counted as dropped); the optional live flag with a `read` callback (items 2 and 3 of the request) was not built, the push stream replaces it; a stream attached to sources of two different worlds is not detected (only within one world); while the mixer starts a read after a long backlog a concurrent push can drop its own newest frames instead of the oldest (counted); frames pushed before a voice starts on the stream wait in the ring (up to `capacity_frames`, 200 ms by default) and are played first, so a host that feeds a stream long before its source starts hears old audio (set a small `capacity_frames` or start the voice first); `audio_stream_push` takes the engine's control lock for the lookup, so a long control call delays it; a voice that plays a push stream makes `audio_snapshot` fail with `UNSUPPORTED`; the equality of the live and the clip voice is checked by RMS of 9 blocks at five places, not by the zipper noise of a moving entity or by Doppler (not measured); the 200 ms underrun test counts at least 30 underruns rather than an exact number.

- **Slot UV transform (maintenance, 2026-11-18, Linux; `plans/maintenance/host-slot-uv-transform.md`):** the fitting cases of `host_acceptance_scene` (4:3 and 9:16 pictures on the 16:9 screen) ran on Linux Vulkan, Direct3D 12 and Vulkan on the Windows PC (2026-11-18) and Metal on the Mac (2026-11-19: 4:3 gives 13, 82, 13 columns of bar, picture, bar; 9:16 gives 37, 34, 37), with pixel checks by hue on one row through the screen, not a golden image; the column counts were not compared between the APIs (Linux and the PC did not record theirs). The bars are a hard edge (no antialiasing at the picture's border); the mask uses the texture's size, so a host texture resized between frames takes effect on the next frame. Minted material blobs made before this change have a 48-byte parameter block where the template now has 80 bytes (not tested what loading one does; the importer and `asset_mint` mint the 80-byte block).

- **X11 content scale (2026-11-11, Linux machine):** `X11Window` now reports the desktop's `Xft.dpi` over 96 as `ContentScale()` and the window host opens X11 windows at the logical size times it. Checked here on one display (Xwayland, `Xft.dpi: 192`, scale 2.0, a 320x241 logical request opens 640x482) and by unit tests of the parser. **Not checked:** the demos by eye on a scaled X11 desktop (`--backend x11` on `ui_demo` and `renderer_demo`); the live change of the scale (the `PropertyNotify` on the root window's `RESOURCE_MANAGER` reloading it), because the test would have to rewrite the property of the running session; a real X11 session (not Xwayland); a fractional `Xft.dpi` such as 120 or 144.
Automated checks pass; nobody has looked at the real thing yet (from `STATUS.md`, Phases 1-2).

- Phase 14 Day 176 (2026-11-09): FreeType 2.15.0's auto-hinter dereferences a null hash table (`fthash.c` `hash_bucket`, reached from `FT_Load_Glyph` with the light or normal hinting mode) when an allocation fails during the first glyph load of a face; seen by allocation-failure injection (`FontLibrary::FailAllocationsAfter`) in `tests/text/font_test.cpp`, ASan SEGV. Opening a font and `LoadOutline` (no hinting) survive failures and leak nothing, and `Font::Open` reports `OutOfMemory` when FreeType swallowed a refused allocation; hinted glyph loading under an allocation failure is not guarded. Not investigated further.

- Phase 14 limits, filed at the phase close (2026-11-09; each is documented in `docs/guide/text.md`, "Limits"): there is **no complex-script shaping** and no right-to-left or vertical text (Arabic and Indic draw isolated forms; HarfBuzz is not committed to, `PLAN.md` assumption #17); **no atlas eviction** (a full atlas, up to 8 pages of 1024 px, drops glyphs and counts them; a dropped glyph is re-rasterized and refused again every frame, there is no negative cache); the largest rasterized size is 128 px (larger text is scaled up from it, blurry, until the 3D text project); **no word movement** in the cursor API (Phase 15); kerning comes from the `kern` table only (GPOS-only fonts get none); no LCD/subpixel rendering; CJK and emoji coverage is whatever the shipped font has (Liberation Sans has neither: `.notdef` boxes); a changed atlas page is uploaded whole (above).

## Deferred items

- **Double precision Day 3 (2026-11-23, Linux): `WorldTransform::matrix` keeps the narrowed translation; stored transforms are version 2.** `ecs::LocalTransform::position` and `WorldTransform::position` are `f64`; `matrix` has the position narrowed so code that wants a matrix reads it (each reader that needs the exact place reads `position`; the series' later days moved the renderer sync, spatial, physics, scripts, audio and the C API). `ecs::LocalTransform`'s stored version is 2 and the oldest 2: stores and levels written before it are refused until re-minted.
- **Double precision Day 8 (2026-11-23, Linux): script demo hash re-baselined on Linux only, and value record version 2.** `kCrossMachineHash` in `tests/script_demo/script_demo_test.cpp` is now 0x0c091fc4195f2f75 (script vectors are `f64`); confirm on the Windows PC and the Mac. Script value records written by earlier builds (version 1) are refused.
- **Double precision Days 6 and 7 (2026-11-23, Linux): physics hashes re-baselined on Linux only.** The physics golden table, the step, joint and hinge hash constants, the C physics sample's checksum, the render-features demo hash and the script demo's cross-machine hash (`kCrossMachineHash`, first taken on the Windows PC) have new values taken on Linux; they must be run on the Windows PC and the Mac (both compilers there) to confirm equal values.

The demos other than `renderer_demo` have no `--origin-offset` yet (below). **The PC, in part (2026-11-23):** the four Windows trees build clean after MSVC's `C4244` (`f64` to `f32`, which clang-cl does not report) was fixed in about 40 places, each made an explicit cast that keeps the value the Linux build computes (`game/physics_demo/physics_scene.cpp`, `tests/spatial/{dynamic_bvh,world_component}_test.cpp`, `spatial_bench.cpp`, `tests/physics/{height_field_collision,height_field,joint,sync,ccd,query,stress}_test.cpp`, `tests/script_physics/*`, `tests/engine_library/host_{character_world,import,physics,physics_joints,script_character}.c`, `surface_input_test.cpp`; `query_index_test.cpp`'s `SameBits` gained an `f64` overload so it still compares every bit), and a unity clash in `tests/rhi/cube_texture_test.cpp` (`kSize` renamed). Plain `build-msvc` over the labels physics, spatial, rhi, renderer, scene, engine_library, shaders, asset, script_physics, boundary and docs, each test capped at 60 s: 1705 of 1720 pass; the 15 failures are the terrain tests on Direct3D 12 and the lighting DXIL (the Phase 28 rows below) and one Debug test over the cap (`HashGrid: 100,000 random operations ...`, 60 s; it passed uncapped). The physics golden table, the C API samples and the far-from-origin tests passed in that run; their hashes were not compared by hand; asan, clang-cl runs and `--affected` were not run. **The Mac (2026-11-23, `build-macos`, plain):** built clean (unity, plain). A demo-side offset for each is a small follow-up if wanted.
- **Double precision series review (2026-11-23, Linux): limits recorded, not fixed.** (1) `spatial::HashGrid` indexes cells with 21-bit keys (`kCellLimit` 2^20): at the default 4 m cell, items beyond about 4.2e6 m from the origin are refused by `Insert` and `Move`; the BVH sets have no such limit. (2) The C API's character set (`engine_c/runtime.cpp`, the character creation) builds a character at a position narrowed to `f32`: a character far from the origin starts at a quantised place. (3) The `Vec3d` field decoders (`asset` level blobs, `storage`) accept NaN, infinity and values beyond 1e12, as the `f32` decoders accepted NaN and infinity; only the importer and the C API check the limit. (4) The physics ray, overlap and shape-cast queries reject a non-finite origin but not one beyond 1e12 (they find nothing). (5) Missing tests: a version-1 `LEVL` blob and a version-1 `local_transform` record refused; the 1e12 refusal of `body_apply_force`, `body_apply_impulse`, `body_teleport`, the two overlaps and `component_set`; a second re-base, a point light and a terrain after a re-base; a CCD hit at 1e8 m. (6) `SceneLink::origin` must be changed through `scene::UpdateRenderOrigin` (the sync notices a re-base by `generation`).
- **Double precision Day 10 (2026-11-23, Linux): the C API's positions are `double` in place; `physics_sample`'s golden re-baselined on Linux only.** `engine_c.h` and `engine.h` changed types (no struct growth, no `64` twins; the ABI version numbers are unchanged, the only client is rebuilt). `tests/engine_library/physics_sample.c` hashes the position doubles: `kGoldenChecksum` is now `0xf013d004` (was `0x7e65470f`); Windows and macOS have not confirmed it. - **Phase 28 Day 501 (2026-11-24, Linux): instances in the scene, the C API and scripts; recorded limits.** (1) The C API draws sets and trees with the surface's one default instanced material (grey, coloured by each instance's tint): there is no material resource for an instanced object, and no mesh-slot, LOD or stand-in data in `engine_tree_node` (the C API's tree has no `TreeLod`). (2) fixed (maintenance Day 04: a state remembers its tree and is reset). (3) A script cannot learn the id of an instance it adds (the add is a command); it moves and removes the instances whose ids the host gave it. The swing of `tree.set_local` is not read back by the script test (the C API has no tree read), only the instances are hashed. - **Phase 28 Day 500 (2026-11-24, Linux): hierarchy culling and levels of detail, with recorded limits.** (1) fixed (the builder refuses a coarser level or stand-in outside the node's own box). (2) Roots are the unit of parallelism (16 roots a job): one huge tree is culled on one thread. (3) `CompactTreeNodes` walks to each node's root (cost proportional to the depth). (4) fixed (the state is reset for another tree). (5) Nodes culled by the frustum keep a stale state entry until they are seen again (dropping it would walk the skipped subtree; declined). (6) The million-node form of the work test is the `soak` ctest (1,015,300 nodes: 3,640 entries looked at, 1,889 emitted, 0.45 ms in a Debug build). Not run on the PC or the Mac: `tests/renderer/instance_tree_cull_test.cpp`.
- **Phase 28 Day 499 (2026-11-24, Linux): the instance tree, with recorded limits.** (1), (2) fixed (maintenance Day 04: one group per marked subtree; `Attach` and `Detach` move and repair only the span and the ancestors). (3) The builder's 2^22-node refusal (`Full`) has no test (4 million nodes of about 270 bytes is over a gigabyte). (4) `Detach` keeps a node's world position, rotation and scale as composed by `Compose` (no shear); a parent with a non-uniform scale over a rotated child cannot be represented exactly, as in `math/transform.h`. (5) Each node record is about 270 bytes (two transforms, two boxes, a root origin field on every node); a million-node tree is about 270 MB; the side table for the root origin was not done. (6) The tree is not yet in the ECS, culled or drawn (Days 500 and 501). Not run on the PC or the Mac: `tests/renderer/instance_tree_test.cpp`, `tests/asset/instance_tree_blob_test.cpp`.

(4) `Scatter` takes a plain function pointer for the height, not the terrain's query directly; no test runs it on a real terrain. Not run on the PC or the Mac: the new `tests/asset/instance_blob_test.cpp`, `tests/scene/instanced_sync_test.cpp`.
- **Phase 28 Day 497 (2026-11-24, Linux): the instanced draw, with recorded limits and deviations.** (1) `gbuffer.vert` has a fifth module (`INSTANCED`, key 4) and the SPIR-V of all five is re-minted (the source changed), but the committed DXIL of the first four no longer matches their SPIR-V and the DXIL of module 4 is absent (`kGbufferVertPerm4Dxil` is a null placeholder added by hand to `gbuffer.vert.dxil.inc`; no dxc here), and the Metal library file was replaced by the no-Mac placeholder (the old Metal bytes matched the old SPIR-V, so none would have stayed valid): the Windows PC mints the DXIL (Day 502) and the Mac the Metal libraries (Day 503); until then every G-buffer vertex module fails the DXIL and Metal minted-library tests there (`tests/renderer/shader_dxil_test.cpp`, `shader_metallib_test.cpp`; module 4 added to both, its recorded fnv is 0), Direct3D 12 and Metal cannot build G-buffer vertex pipelines from the committed bytes and the pass refuses instanced objects there (the object index stream is the test). (2) Deviation from the day file: the visible instances are compacted each frame into a mapped per-frame buffer in `FrameResources` (`kDefaultFrameInstances` = 262,144 instances; the rest are counted in `GBufferStats::instances_dropped`, not a setting yet) instead of a persistent per-set GPU buffer with per-chunk dirty upload and per-run draws; the cost is a CPU compose of every visible instance every frame (about 64 bytes written each), the gain no GPU-side state to rebuild on a reset. The per-chunk versions of Day 495 are therefore unused by the renderer for now. (3) The set's own transform is a position, a rotation and a uniform scale (not a matrix); instances are narrowed to `f32` in the set's space (keep the set's origin near its instances; the set's `f64` chunk origins matter only inside the set). (4) `FitCameraDepthRange` and the lighting passes do not see instanced sets. (5) The GPU cost of rebuilding the matrices from the 40-byte record against a `Mat4x3` record was not measured (no timing source in the rig); the shader reads a 64-byte `GpuInstance` (position, quaternion, scale, tint, custom) and rotates by the quaternion. (6) Negative scales are refused by `InstanceSet`, so the winding-flip test of the day file does not apply. **The PC (Day 502, 2026-11-24):** the DXIL of all five `gbuffer.vert` modules is minted (module 4 included), and Direct3D 12 draws instanced sets and trees: an instanced draw binds the object index stream too, which now holds `kObjectIndexStreamEntries` (262,144) indices so instance `i` of a draw reads entry `first_instance + i`, and a frame writes no instance past the stream's end (the rest are counted in `instances_dropped`; null-device test `On a device that reads the object index stream, no instance is written past the stream's end`); the instancing render tests pass on Vulkan and Direct3D 12 on the PC. Open: `gbuffer.vert`'s Metal library is stale again (`Every minted Metal library ...` names `gbuffer.vert` on the PC), so the Mac re-mints it. **The Mac (2026-11-24, `build-macos`, plain):** `gbuffer.vert`'s Metal library is minted (all five modules); the 65 instancing tests, `engine_c_instances` and the scene tests pass on Metal, validated, with the PC's index-stream change for instanced draws (the same fix the Mac had made in parallel, dropped at the merge).
- **Phase 28 Day 487 (2026-11-23, Linux): (the `test_phase_script_long_warning` flake is fixed: two ctests wrote the same scratch files beside the JUnit fixture). The terrain tiles are not in the spatial `StaticBvh` and `terrain.*` are not registered settings yet (Day 490).
- **Phase 28 Day 488 (2026-11-23, Linux): a human has not looked at the seams.** The seam is now tested by the morph factors on both sides of every edge between levels (`terrain tiles: the seams between levels ...`), not by pictures; a visual check of the seams from a low oblique camera, with a cliff, has not been done by a human. The vertex shader measures the morph distance to the unmorphed vertex, selection uses box distance (the bound in `TerrainMaxMorphFraction` covers the difference on flat maps).
- **Phase 28 Day 489 (2026-11-23, Linux): the terrain material is smaller than the day file listed.** Built as the design spec says: 4 layers (albedo and roughness in one `RGBA8_SRGB` array of 2 to 4 layers), a splat map, a slope rule and a distance fade. Not built: 8 layers (a second splat map), normal layers, height-based blending, triplanar sampling and a `.terrain.src` source (the textures go through the existing texture minter, and `asset::NormalizeSplatImage` normalises the splat pixels; no `asset_mint` command calls it yet, Day 493 with the C API). The fade distances are fields of `TerrainMaterial`, not a registered setting (Day 493 registers the `terrain.*` settings). Nobody has looked at the layer blend on screen; the GPU tests compare colours at probe pixels.
- **Phase 28 Day 490 (2026-11-23, Linux): terrain in the scene, with recorded limits.** Moved to Day 493: spatial registration and tile-bound ray queries, the level reference and `level_spawner` HGHT loader, the `terrain.*` settings (`SceneFrame::SetTerrainSelect` takes the values meanwhile). Limits: the sync notices a changed source pointer, flags or world, not an in-place material edit; tiles past 4096 a frame (all terrains together) are dropped farthest first and counted (`SceneFrame::TerrainTilesDropped`); a terrain the render scene refuses is retried every sync pass; eight terrains a frame at most. DXIL and Metal terrain shaders are still placeholders. Nobody has looked at two terrains on screen.
- **Phase 28 Day 491 (2026-11-23, Linux): the height-field collider, with recorded limits.** (Rewritten by the maintenance series' Day 03.) A shape whose box covers more than 1024 triangles still collides with the first 256 listed (the lowest-z rows): a 20 m box on a 1 m grid gets contacts from the first rows only. An attempt to collide wide shapes in blocks failed: the cores' search (GJK/EPA) gives long exits for a thin triangle inside a wide shape, and the internal-edge rule and the infinite-plane exit of a ramp triangle then push a wide box the wrong way (a box on a plateau fell through in the experiment); it needs a column-based contact for fields that is not built. The ridge push-out (a resting sphere at a crest with both slopes over 45 degrees can be pushed along the far face) is the same family and not changed. A shape cast of a field is refused. Not run on the PC or the Mac: the new `height_field.*`, `height_field_test.cpp` and `terrain_collider_test.cpp`.
- **Host texture layouts (maintenance, 2026-11-24, Linux; `plans/maintenance/host-texture-pixel-formats.md`): not built or run on the PC or the Mac.** `include/engine/engine_c.h` (`ENGINE_UPDATE_BGRA`, `ENGINE_UPDATE_IGNORE_ALPHA`, `ENGINE_FORMAT_*`, `texture_format_support` appended to the table), `engine.h`, `engine/engine_c/host_texture.*`, `abi/engine_api.snapshot`, `tests/engine_library/{host_import.c,abi_snapshot_gen.c}`. `engine/engine_c/live_textures.*` makes a `BGRA8` device texture for a host that sends B, G, R, A (not run on Direct3D 12 or Metal; `FormatSupport` guards it, and a device that refuses falls back to a swap in the staging copy). `ENGINE_UPDATE_IGNORE_ALPHA` is a CPU pass writing 255 (the RHI has no component swizzle), so the query answers CONVERTED for it.
- **Maintenance `linux-known-issues` Day 08 (2026-11-24, Linux): findings of the series review that are recorded, not fixed.** (1) `TerrainHeightFn` (`engine/engine_c/runtime.cpp`) decides a child terrain is not placed yet from an identity world transform under a non-zero local offset: a child whose parent's transform cancels its offset exactly reports NOT_READY, and a rotated child with a zero offset before the first frame is queried unturned. (2) `MusicPlayer::Post` (`engine/audio/music.cpp`) retries a refused command: a retried `Fade` is re-queued one frame later, and a later segment of the same slot posted in the same update can land first (a fade-in can stop below 1); a retried `Start` keeps its in-block frame offset in a later block; fade segments due beside a refused `Start` are dropped. (3) `EnvironmentUsable` does not check `sky_rotation` (its two callers do). (4) `TerrainTiles::Balance` now repeats until stable; the pattern the review described (an emitted split tile two levels from a neighbour) did not occur in 480 random selections, with or without the change, so no test shows it. (5) A second `audio.play{entity=e}` forgets the first handle without an `on_voice_end`.
- **Phase 28 Day 505 (2026-11-24, Linux): findings of the uncapped phase review that are recorded, not fixed.** Terrain (the rest after the maintenance series' Day 01): (1) the morph's seam guarantee holds for flat tiles; a tall box (a cliff) can still start the coarser tile's morph before the finer tile's far corner, so a crack is possible beside a cliff (`TerrainMaxMorphFraction` is a flat-map bound; the neighbour-level rule itself holds for any heights); (2) the height map's loader checks the shape of the pyramid and mips, not their values (`asset::VerifyHeightMap` does, in tools and tests); (3) for a map whose side is not a power of two (1025 is the common one) the vertex shader's uv for a coarse mip drifts by up to one texel of that mip at the far edge (a change to `terrain.vert.slang` would have to be re-minted for DXIL and Metal by the PC and the Mac for a sub-texel error); (4) the physics height-field library of a world keeps a field per replaced height map (it has no remove; the pins and the surface's sources are released), and `height_map_create` of an 8192 x 8192 map still holds the host's samples as little-endian bytes, the mip chain and the blob, then the table's copy, at once (the peak is not measured), but a failed allocation of the first buffer is now `LIMIT_EXCEEDED`; (5) a child terrain is "not ready" by the identity of its world transform under a non-zero local offset, not by a flag. Physics: (7) a body larger than about 12 rows of cells gets contacts only from the first 256 triangles (the lowest-z rows); (8) fixed (Day 03); (9) near a sharp crest (both slopes steeper than 45 degrees) a resting sphere can be pushed out along the far face; (10) and (11) fixed (Day 03). Instances: (12) and (13) fixed (maintenance Day 04); (14) a wind node's rotation is replaced by the wind's rotation about x (documented); (15) fixed (an unchanged chunk origin keeps the offsets; a chunk cap and a distance rule). C API: (16) `instance_set_set_transform` overwrites the tint with the item's; the call can apply the transform and then fail on the data; (17) and (18) fixed (maintenance Day 05: the minter's faces path and the prefilter refuse an infinity or NaN texel, `DeviceCaps::max_texture_dimension_cube` is checked by the null and Vulkan devices; the minter already refused them through the mip chain, so (17) only gained a test); (19) `ENGINE_ABI_VERSION_MINOR` was not raised for the struct changes of the double-precision series (the user decided on 2026-11-24, maintenance `linux-known-issues` Day 08, to leave it: the only client is rebuilt; `script_unload` stays unbuilt for that reason). Fixed on this day (regression tests for the `Attach` offset, the stand-in rule, the scatter rule, the height map header, the cell size, the ray through a diagonal and the changed height-field id; the others are checked by the existing suites only): the abort through `environment.set` (the surface falls back to the flat colour), height maps and fields with an extreme range or cell size, unused terrain layers poisoning the material, `Attach` dropping a root's offset, a stand-in beyond the cull distance, a stale LOD state in a reused slot, a hostile scatter rule, a ray through a cell diagonal, the physics sync ignoring a changed height-field id, and a failed sky environment cube rebuilt every frame.

  - (4) Deferred from series Day 6:
    - bind groups are not cached by contents: the UI renderer makes one per texture switch and the text renderer one per atlas page per frame, and a
      Vulkan `CreateBindGroup` makes four heap allocations. It needs a reset-safe cache tested with the golden pictures;

  - (7) Seen on series Day 13, reworked the same day: `Sort` is now pattern-defeating quicksort, many times faster on sorted, reversed,
    all-equal and few-distinct input and 22% faster on random 56-byte elements, but about 7% slower on random `u32` than the old introsort. The
    G-buffer's draw sort no longer uses `qsort` (a `StableSort` of 24-byte proxies, as fast or faster).
  - (8) Not run at the series close (2026-11-19): the `linux-nojit` tree, `-DENGINE_BUILD_SHARED=ON`, `-DENGINE_LIBRARY_WITH_IMPORTER=OFF` and the

- **Phase 27 Day 468 (2026-11-19, Linux):** deferred or not done: (1) `audio.play` takes `position` and `entity` since 2026-11-24 (maintenance `linux-known-issues` Day 07 follow-up; `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-11-24-script-audio-placement.md`); `AudioSource` itself is still not a script-visible component (it holds shared clip pointers, which a stored component cannot), so the clip-by-name component of that spec's option C is not built. (2) The plan names `audio.music.play/crossfade/stop`; the binding registry has no dotted namespaces, so they are `audio.play_music`, `audio.crossfade_music` and `audio.stop_music`. (3) The platform layer has no gamepad type and no action-mapping system: the host fills `InputMap` and passes stick values as `ExternalAxis`; a key pressed and released between two captures is not seen; `on_input` carries no gamepad events. - **Crash handler chaining Day 3 (2026-11-19, the Mac): done; limits and what it left.** (1) **A local change to the vendored Breakpad** (`third_party/sentry_native/VENDORED.md`, "Local changes"; the user's decision): a switch the engine sets during a crash, only when the host has a handler for the crash's signal, keeps Breakpad from `_exit`ing after the dump, resumes the threads it suspended and forwards the exception itself. A pin update must carry it over; nothing checks that it is still there except the chaining tests. (2) **The ports from before Breakpad:** with chaining on, the exception goes to the task's exception ports from before `sentry_init` (a debugger's, another crash reporter's), or back to the kernel when there are none; only the "none" case is tested. (3) **Without a host handler for the crash's signal nothing changes**: the process still ends with `_exit(<exception type>)` (exit code 1 for an access fault, 5 for an abort), not a signal, unlike Linux. With one, it dies of the signal. (4) **Which signal a fault becomes is the kernel's choice**: an access fault is relayed on `SIGSEGV` and `SIGBUS` alike; a crash whose thread is not found arms every hardware signal the host has a handler for. (5) **Catch2's own fatal-signal handlers** are earlier handlers in a death-test child (the child runs inside a test case), so every macOS crash test now chains to them after the dump; the no-chaining test puts `SIGSEGV` and `SIGBUS` back to the default first. (6) Not run: asan and tsan (the Mac's rule), so the relay was never run under a sanitizer here; the dump-failure case of Day 1 has no macOS test; arm64 compile-only. (7) The day's runs had the console locked, so they ran with `--no-display`. (8) From the review: the host-handler code (note, classify, call) is a near copy of Linux's (`os/linux/debug/crash_signal.cpp`), and the two already differed once (Linux re-raised `SIGTRAP`; fixed here); a shared POSIX helper would change Linux code this machine cannot build. (9) The relay clears the task's fault ports before the host's handler runs, so ports a debugger or another crash reporter had before the engine are not used for a fault inside that handler or after it returns. (10) After the review's fixes only the crash tests, phase 7 and the C host were run again, not the whole scoped run.
- **Crash handler chaining Day 1 (2026-11-19, Linux): The host's request, done on Linux; what it left.** (1) **Windows and macOS are done** (Days 2 and 3, 2026-11-19): see their entries; `crash_host.c` checks the chaining on all three (`ENGINE_HOST_HANDLER_CHAINED`). (2) Under AddressSanitizer with `handle_segv=1`, ASan's own `SIGSEGV` handler counts as the host's and runs after the dump (its report, its exit code); `engine_c_runtime_asan` runs with `handle_segv=0`. (3) The dump-failure test passes with and without the review's fix (a host handler that raises ends the process at its first call either way), so it does not prove the fix. (4) Seen once: in the first of three tsan runs of `debug_tests "[crash]"` after the fix, a second crash test besides the known stack-overflow one failed; its name was not kept, and the next two runs failed only the known one.
- **Query and hierarchy indices Day 2 (2026-11-19, Linux): the ECS children index; open.**
  (1) **Windows** has not built or run it (`engine/ecs/world.*`, `transform.*`, `tests/ecs/hierarchy_index_test.cpp`). **The Mac** built and
  ran it on 2026-11-19 (plain): the `Hierarchy index` cases and the script world hashes pass.
  (2) **Removing a child scans its siblings**, so clearing every child of a parent with k children one by one is O(k²) (it was O(N) per call
  before).
  (3) **`World::ComponentVersion` does not count writes made by hand into an archetype's columns** (`ArchetypeAt`); every caller outside the
  ECS only reads through it.
  (4) **`World::StorageOrder` packs the archetype into 24 bits, the chunk into 20 and the row into 20**; nothing checks a world that outgrows
  them.

  (1) **Windows** has not built or run it (`engine/physics/query_index.*`, `query.cpp`, `world.*`, `step.cpp`, `snapshot.cpp`,
  `tests/physics/query_index_test.cpp`). **The Mac** built and ran it on 2026-11-19 (plain): the four `Query index` cases and the phase 25
  golden and determinism hashes pass.
  (2) **The step pays for the index** in every world, whether it queries or not: +0.53 to 0.56 ms per step at 5,000 falling bodies on one
  thread (17.5 to 18.0 ms), +0.3 ms at rest, and 37.4 to 41.3 ms of a 712 ms step at 50,000 bodies (`physics_bench`). A lazy refresh was
  declined: a `const` query cannot catch up, so a world queried without stepping would scan.
  (3) **`ClosestPoint`'s candidates** are the colliders whose box is within twice the distance, which rests on the narrow phase's gap being
  accurate to within the distance; the oracle found no difference, but it is not a proof.
  (4) **A pose or shape written through `Find`/`FindCollider`** instead of `SetTransform`/`SetCollider` is not followed; only builds with slow
  checks detect it.
  (5) **Flakes seen in the day's runs**, each passing on rerun: `script_bench_check` (plain, under load) and `LastSaveStatus can be read while
  another thread saves` (plain, phase 11).

- **Phase 27 Day 477 (2026-11-19, Linux): findings of the uncapped phase review that were recorded, not fixed (the fixed ones are in the day file).** Script sandbox and determinism: (1) `pairs`/`next` order over hashed keys differs between runs (LuaJIT seeds its string hash from the OS; tables keyed by tables or functions hash by address), so a script that lets that order reach the world breaks the identical-state guarantee: the guide says to sort keys; `tostring(table)` shows an address. (2) The behavior host (`__behavior_call`, `behavior`, `event`) is installed as ordinary globals that a script can replace for its own state. (3) Instruction and time budgets are checked every 1,000 VM instructions, so one C-library call (`("x"):rep(2e8)`, `upper`, `table.sort` of millions) runs to its end first. (4) `print` writes to the log directly: no `script.log_limit`, no partition order. (5) The metatables of engine arrays, maps and bytes are writable by scripts of the same state. (6) `Value::Set` and `Find` scan linearly: a 65,000-key map costs seconds to marshal. (7) A few destructors of heap objects stay alive over a Lua call that may raise (a memory-cap error then leaks them). (8) `static_cast<f32>(double)` of a value over the float range in two bindings. The `expr` test backend has no memory limit, burns CPU on many globals, keeps definitions of a failed load, and compares integers above 2^53 as doubles (test-only language). Core and bindings: conflict detection keys on (entity, command kind), not the component, so two partitions writing different components of one entity count as a conflict (and `add` against `set` is missed); a placeholder handle kept across ticks names whatever the next tick's `spawn` reserved; the command overlay is a linear scan (quadratic in distinct writes of one callback); the command caps (2^24 commands, 1 GiB payload per partition) are far above a hostile script's need; a handler that parks in `wait_event` of its own event wakes in the same tick; a native event handler that unsubscribes during delivery; a debugger breakpoint line is truncated to 32 bits; `ApplySpawn` and `ApplyComponentAdd` leave a default entity or component when the merge-time store fails; `PartitionContext::replay_recording` is never set by the runtime (`time.real` is not refused while a replay records). Physics, audio and input bindings: forces, impulses and velocities are checked for finiteness, not magnitude (1e30 overflows to inf in the step); `physics.shape_cast` and `overlap_box` do not check the rotation quaternion; audio handles are guessable and not checked for ownership (and share `Reserve()` with `entity.spawn`); queries sort the whole matching set before applying `limit`; a recorded replay can hold values `Deserialize` refuses (a NaN pointer, over 2^24 ticks). C API: `component.define` takes a process-wide ECS type slot that is never freed (about four worlds that each define components exhaust 128); `script_load`, `register_function`, `set_budget`, `stats`, `event_subscribe` and `event_unsubscribe` block on the world mutex, so a call from inside an event callback or a host function deadlocks (documented as forbidden, not detected). Day 477 fixed: the task owners of dropped instances (a sort), the random stream of a resumed wait, a resume of a failed instance, `time.frame`, the C API's default budget (it was unlimited), the order of the `struct_size` check in `script_task_run`, the chunk-count overflow, and a cap of 16 MiB on the strings and blobs of one value pool.
- **Phase 27 Day 473 (2026-11-19, the Windows PC): LuaJIT, the scripts and the directory watcher on Windows; not done or seen.** (1) **The script cross-machine check passes on the Mac too** (2026-11-19, Apple clang with the JIT, plain; and the nojit tree's `script_demo_tests`): `tests/script_demo`'s `Script demo: the state hash after 150 frames is the same on every machine` commits the PC's value (`0xCD4A4433E698FAD7`; MSVC and clang-cl, Debug and Release, JIT and no JIT), and Linux passes it (2026-11-19, Clang with the JIT, plain, asan and tsan); a machine that fails it has found a real divergence. `script_demo --frames 120` also ends on `e1f5c33055210a41` on all three machines. The one Linux value on record, the C API sample's `e3e3ca13a45686f9` (Day 472), the PC gives too. (2) **A Debug build on Windows runs scripts far slower than Linux's and barely scales:** `script_demo` takes 1,145 ms of script time a frame at 1 thread and 851 ms at 16 (Release: 261 ms and 28.6 ms); observed with every Lua allocation going through the `/MDd` C runtime heap, not profiled further. `engine_c_script_sample_asan` took 589 s alone (59 s plain) and timed out at 600 s in a parallel run; its Windows asan timeout is now 1800 s. (3) The crash hook's vectored observer records every first-chance exception, and every Lua error on Windows is one (LuaJIT raises SEH exceptions), so a script raising errors on many threads at once can push a fault out of the 128-entry ring before the report reads it; the report then names the thread by its stack pointer. (4) Not run on Windows: the crash-test label (left out of the PC runs as before), the reset-loop test of phase 8 (the user's rule), the windowed `script_demo` (not built on any machine). (5) Seen failing once in the day's full parallel runs and passing alone: in `build-clang-cl` plain, `Windows: the mouse fills position, buttons with capture, and the wheel in the Linux units` and `engine_c_acceptance_scene` (each 3 of 3 alone); in `build-msvc` asan, `engine_c_runtime_asan` timed out at 1800 s (the hang of the Phase 23 Day 354 entry; 0.4 s alone).

The surface, texture, glTF import, resource registry and pixel-read wrappers and the crash-handler pair are compiled (warnings as errors, clang unity, GCC, no-unity, shared, Release) but no C++ program calls them; only the C programs run those functions of the table. Not built on the Windows PC or the Mac.
- **Phase 26 closing review (Day 443, 2026-11-18, Linux): findings declined or deferred** (also: `Reconfigure` keeps rate-dependent effect state; a virtual voice ignores `Pause`; long `Fade` snapshots exceed `kMaxRampLength`; a snapshot's limiter check and `Snapshot: the cost` test are timing- or bound-sensitive under load) (fixed ones are in the Day 443 file). Backends: the COM apartment of the WASAPI device is entered in `Open` and left in `Release`, which may run on another thread; a retry from the fallback state blocks the control thread while PipeWire or ALSA open (up to about 3 s) and the null clock loses that time; the PipeWire loader's `g_loaded`/`g_api` are unsynchronised (enumerators are free functions). Formats and streams: `MintOgg` does not check decoded samples for finiteness or decode kept Ogg bytes to their granule length at mint time; `Vector::Reserve` aborts on exhaustion in `DecodeWav`, `asset_clip.cpp` and the minter instead of returning `OutOfMemory`, and an exhausted heap is reported as `TooShort`/`BadSamples` in places; `StreamBuffer::Refill` ignores a failed `Seek`; a one-frame loop makes a Vorbis refill seek-bound; `MakeWavFileStream` parses more loosely than `ParseWav` (no `block_align` check, last `fmt ` chunk, no `smpl` loops); `StreamPump::Update` queues a job per call while one is pending; `SkippedPackets()` counts one per seek or loop wrap. Snapshots and C API: `Mixer::LoadState` does not check floats for finiteness, does not cross-check bus sends and ducking (cycles) or the control slots against the voices, and writes frame counts as `u32`; `audio_render` takes the runtime lock through `CurrentRuntime()` although the header promises it does not, and `audio_close` does not wait for calls in flight; every `audio_bus_effect_set` uses one of the 64 permanent effect slots; `SpatialAudio::Drop` forgets an emitter when its `Stop` is refused by a full queue, a looping emitter whose voice was stolen is marked finished for good, and a velocity is not reset when only the radius changes. Each is real as read; none was reproduced by a failing test.

Phase 26 alone at `2e30744f`: `ran 174 of 10010 tests (phases: 26; flavors: plain; display: included; crash tests: excluded) in 11 s`, the same three hashes failed; Days 410-411 compared the hashes and ran their scoped tests. (2) Observed, not investigated: `engine_c_surface` fails in the Release tree (`build-rel`, 4 of 4 runs: `host_surface: line 326: CHECK failed: g_opens_from_program == 1`) and passes in the Debug tree; the three `engine_c_import_shared*` tests fail and hang (killed after 29 minutes) in a `-DENGINE_BUILD_SHARED=ON -DENGINE_LIBRARY_WITH_IMPORTER=OFF` tree (`import_gltf returned 8 (unsupported)`); the physics tests of that tree pass. (3) Review findings filed, not fixed: **narrow phase** `MeshPair` tests only the first 256 triangles of a box query (a big body over dense terrain can miss contacts); the GJK/EPA simplex thresholds are absolute (hulls and cores under about 10 cm across fall back to the centre contact); EPA drops faces past 192 silently and a GJK that hits its iteration cap uses stale weights; a hostile hull or mesh blob passes `Open` without a plane/winding check; `MergeContacts` relies on its callers capping at 64. **Dynamics** CCD ignores `collide_connected == false` joints; edits to a live `Joint` component are not applied (create-only); two contacts of one manifold with equal feature ids make a snapshot fail to load; absolute epsilons switch joint rows off for bodies of mass about 1e10 or inertia about 1e12; a body jointed only to a slow kinematic body can flip between asleep and awake; `ContactSolver::ForgetBody` is dead code; the joint-exclusion scan in the step is linear per pair. **Queries, character, events** shape casts are sampled, not swept (a cast longer than 1024 samples tunnels thin things); the character controller's `Move` leaves the position to the next step (two moves between steps overwrite each other); a group/mask change while a pair persists can give a begin without an end; an end event for a removed trigger reports `trigger=false`; destroying a body gives no end event to its partners; a resting pair that newly meets (layer or mask change) gets no first narrow test until something moves; non-finite poses give NaN boxes. **Snapshots** the state hash omits joints, the warm-start cache, contacts and sleep timers, so the snapshot tests cannot prove the snapshot complete; the input recorder covers 7 mutators only and the class comment now says what a replay covers; `Restore` does not check hull-library identity, contact/trigger sort order or entity uniqueness, a blob of dead slots can allocate about 60 times its size, and a `Restore` right before an input that wakes neighbours differs from the original run until one step has rebuilt the broad phase; the floating-point flag check scans only the global flag variables and exact tokens (`-ffp-model=fast`, per-target options and `/clang:` spellings pass). **C API** body creation order depends on when the host called a query (out-of-frame `SyncBodies`), so the same scene can hash differently; destroying the entity of a `Joint` component leaves the physics joint; a `Restore` can leave a ghost capsule or duplicate bodies when the entities' links differ from the snapshot's; the joint component skips `Joint::Validate` (a refused joint stays pending without an error); ray and overlap results report entity 0 for a character's capsule; a frame without a step clears the previous frame's events; `physics_events` needs every element's `struct_size` set. Each is a limit or a gap with a recorded scenario, not a known crash; none changes a committed hash.

- Phase 21 Day 290 (2026-11-11, the Mac): libbacktrace reads executables, dylibs and dSYMs but not `MH_BUNDLE` images (`macho.c`), so a library built as a CMake `MODULE` on macOS gets unnamed frames. - **Phase 15 Day 206 (2026-11-09, Linux machine): the end-of-phase review found more than the phase fixed; these are filed, not fixed** (each is verified by reading the code, none by a failing test unless noted). Toolkit: (1) `Surface` holds popup and widget removals until the end of the whole `Frame`, so a popup closed by one event is still hit-testable by the next event of the same batch (a second click in one frame can run an item twice; `TrackPopups` and `FocusNext` still count dying popups). (2) Layout containers re-measure children in `Arrange` without the subtree theme override (`OverrideScope` is pushed only in `MeasureTotal` and `DrawTree`). (3) A modal opened from a menu saves the popup item as the focus to return to, so the text field that had it is not restored. (4) Cancelling a drag (Escape, `CancelDrag`, `ReleasePointer`) clears the button record while the physical button is down, so the later `PointerUp` reaches a widget that saw no `PointerDown`. (5) Ids of unnamed children come from their index and are not recomputed on `RemoveChild`/`BringToFront`, so an id can repeat. (6) `ListBox::SyncModel` clears the selection and current row on any item-count change without telling `OnSelectionChanged`. (7) A text field's `scroll_x_` is only recomputed on edits and caret moves (a `SetText` before layout, or a later widening, leaves it stale); a drop-move inside a field is two edits (two undo steps, and the second can be refused after the first). (8) `MenuView::rows_` is rebuilt only when the item count differs, so changed texts or a count change before the next `Draw` can be indexed stale. (9) `Callback::Invoke` allocates a snapshot per call; `DrawList::SortLayers` is quadratic for many interleaved layers; `SurfaceUpdater` `FixedRate` drops time when a frame covers more than two periods (the UI clock runs slow). (10) `DebugOverlay`/`SettingsView` and the demo format with `snprintf` into fixed buffers (clamped now). Platform: a new Wayland keymap or a lost seat capability forgets held keys without a `KeyUp`; Win32 `WM_CAPTURECHANGED` and `ReleaseInput` zero the buttons without a button-up event; Wayland `ClearImage` can leave the cursor undefined without `wp_cursor_shape_v1`, and a pointer lock does not hide the cursor (**seen by the user on 2026-11-11: with `input_probe` the lock hides the pointer on X11 and leaves it visible on Wayland**); X11 keeps oversized foreign clipboard data on the window and sends `PointerMove` during a lock; Win32 `SetClipboard` empties the clipboard before it knows the set will succeed; Wayland offers can be evicted before use and `wl_data_source_set_actions` is sent below version 3. (The standard-library and `unsigned char` findings that stood here were closed by Phase 16: `std_usage_check` replaced `std_container_check` and scans the `.inc`, `.mm` and `.c` files too.) `tools/ui_widget_style_check` ignores `/* */` comments, short hex literals and `.h` files under `widgets/`, and passes when it finds no file. `renderer_demo`, `rhi_demo`: nothing found. Renderer: nothing enforces one UI pass per frame per `UiRenderer` (a second declaration overwrites the first); `ViewportTarget` does not validate the depth format; `UiSurfaceTarget::NeedsPaint` after a reset depends on handles not repeating; `UiSurfaceTarget::Destroy` leaves `has_resource_` and `frame_resource_` set; `UiRenderer` creates a bind group per texture switch. `ui_demo`: the script parser matches `ctrl+`/`shift+`/`alt+` case-sensitively, has no digit keys, parses `frame:` with `atol`, splits only on spaces and takes a stray continuation byte in `type:` as a lead. `SKIP` inside `FOR_EACH_GPU_BACKEND` in `ui_demo_gpu_test.cpp` ends the remaining backends, and the Wayland "reports a Resize event" test has no assertion that one arrived. Fixed from the same review (not an open item): see the day file.

- Phase 15 Day 206 (2026-11-09, Linux machine), flaky: `X11Window translates key press/release into Key via the server's keymap` (phase 1, plain) failed once in the `--affected` plain run and in 2 of 7 and 2 of 11 runs alone with `ctest -R` (with this day's X11 change reverted for the second series), passing in the rest; it passed in the full run of the same day. Possibly the user's session activity; not investigated.
- Phase 15 Day 204 (2026-11-09, Linux machine): **no human has used `ui_demo` in a window.** Not seen by eye: both themes at 1x and 2x in a live window, a text field with accents, a menu near a window edge, the viewport resizing while the window edge is dragged, the cursor shapes on hover, the right-button pointer lock orbit on Wayland and X11, drag and drop and the clipboard against a real file manager and editor (only the fake OS peer and the scripted windows ran). The six goldens and the screenshots were looked at by the session only. The golden images come from this machine's GPU (NVIDIA GeForce RTX 3060, Vulkan); Direct3D 12 is not checked against them. On the Windows PC (Day 205) `ui_demo` runs 1000 frames with validation on Direct3D 12 and Vulkan, its scripted runs pass on both, and the goldens pass on the Radeon 860M with both backends. The user saw it in a window there at 200%, where the widgets were half size until the window's DPI was followed (fixed the same day); the rest of the list above was not looked at. `resize:WxH` works offscreen only. The in-world screen's texture is bound to its material just before `RenderFrame`, so a recovery inside a frame leaves it stale for that one frame (the next picture is identical in the test). The viewport shows the Phase 5 demo scene, not a single cube. **Looked at by the user on Wayland (2026-11-11, Linux machine, NVIDIA RTX 3060):** both themes, accents in a text field, menus near the window edge, cursor shapes, the right-button pointer-lock orbit (the pointer stays put while rotating), and dropping a file and pasting text all behaved correctly. **Two observations:** (1) dragging the window edge resizes smoothly but lags behind the mouse, as if every resize or motion event were processed rather than the latest one; not measured, cause not investigated. (2) Changing the three rendering settings (present mode, render scale, shader quality) showed no difference in the picture. Expected for two of them: the renderer README documents render scale and shader quality as having no consumer yet (every apply is logged as done); present mode changes pacing only. Not a defect. **On X11 (the same day, through Xwayland) everything on the list worked, and the resize lag did not appear: the viewport redraws only when the mouse stops moving, where on Wayland it redraws on every motion event (so the lag is Wayland-specific).** Still not looked at: native X11 (not Xwayland), 2x scale in a live window, the viewport near a window edge during a drag, and the rest of the list.

- Phase 5 Day 81: the point-light scale numbers (1 / 100 / 1000 lights at 1080p) are CPU wall time from submit to idle; GPU-only time was not measured because the RHI has no timestamp queries yet.
- Phase 5 Day 84: the optional DXIL step of `shaders_portability` (`dxc`) has not produced a usable result. The `dxc` installed on the development machine (Arch package, 1.10.2605.37) aborts with `assert(HasHungOffUses ...)` inside LLVM on almost every shader, including a one-input vertex shader written by hand; only a shader with no inputs compiled. To be retried with another `dxc` build. On Windows (Phase 13 Day 163) the Windows SDK's `dxc` 1.9.2602.17 compiles every shader and key, and a rejection fails the test there; on Linux it stays a note. Consequence: DXIL is minted on Windows only, so a shader changed and re-minted on Linux keeps its old `*.dxil.inc` until it is minted on Windows; `shader_dxil_test` (the recorded SPIR-V hash) then fails on both machines and names it. The Metal compiler step is not run (needs macOS). The renderer demo on both display backends was run live on 2026-11-05 (Wayland and X11, 1000 frames each, validation and sync validation silent); a locked session breaks that check.
- Phase 5 Day 83: `perf record` here is limited to user-space sampling without call graphs (`kernel.perf_event_paranoid=2`; `-g` and kernel events fail to open), so the 10000-object profile is a flat symbol list. The `renderer_bench` numbers come from one machine (RTX 3060, Ryzen 9 7950X3D).
- Phase 5 Day 82: the demo has no roughness map: the G-buffer fragment shader has no roughness texture input (permutations: normal map, alpha test, vertex color). Roughness and metallic come from material parameters only. Adding a texture input means a shader permutation and a material-layout change.
- Phase 7 Day 92: the AArch64 branches of `engine/foundation/os/posix/debug/fault_injection_posix.cpp` (from Phase 21 Day 291; Linux's file before) (`.inst 0x00000000` for the illegal instruction, `FaultSupported(DivideByZero)` false) have not been compiled or run: no AArch64 machine, and clang cannot cross-compile them here without a sysroot. Day 101's cross-target syntax checks are meant to cover the compile.
- Phase 7 Day 92: the 256 KB per-call bound in the death-test helper's pipe drain (`kDrainBudgetBytes`) has no test that fails without it: on this machine the reader keeps up with three writer threads and sees the pipe empty, so the starvation it guards against could not be reproduced (`A child that writes without pause is still killed at the timeout` passes either way).
- Phase 7 Day 93: `ENGINE_DEBUG_BREAK`'s AArch64 branch (`brk #0xf000`) is not compiled or run here. `IsDebuggerAttached` is true under any tracer (`strace`, `rr`), so an assert failure under one breaks with SIGTRAP instead of aborting (documented on the function).
- Phase 7 Day 94: libraries loaded after `InitDebugFacilities` are not symbolized until `ReloadDebugModules` is called (libbacktrace reads the loaded objects once); their frames show as `??`. Documented in the guide. The Vulkan driver loads its libraries at device creation, so a trace taken inside them needs a reload after that.
- Phase 7 Day 94: the signal-handler allocation test counts `malloc`, `calloc` and `realloc` calls but not `mmap`, which libbacktrace uses for its own memory; the AArch64 behavior of the stack-trace code (unwinder, `dl_iterate_phdr`) is not run here.
- Phase 7 Day 95: the library's own warnings reach the engine log (`LogCrash`) but no test provokes one; routing was confirmed once by raising the level temporarily. The empty-server-address warning is dropped by a text match on "DSN" in `crash_handler_sentry.cpp`, which a library upgrade could invalidate (the silent-install test would then fail).
- Phase 7 Day 97 (2026-11-06): under ThreadSanitizer, a fault inside the crash report (a state provider that crashes) kills the process without the `double fault` message and without a dump (the test skips those two checks in the TSan flavor); plain and ASan print the message and leave the dump. Not investigated.
- Phase 7 Day 97: the report's ten-second budget is checked between pieces, so a state provider that never returns holds the crashing process (a second thread waits up to twenty seconds for it). A watchdog would need a timer signal in the crash path; not done.
- Phase 7 Day 97: the AArch64 branch of the stack-pointer read in `os/linux/debug/crash_signal.cpp` (used to tell a stack overflow from a wild pointer) has not been compiled or run.
- Phase 7 Day 97: the check that a crashing program under `gdb` stops at the original faulting instruction, not inside the recorder, has not been done by a human. (`lldb` opening the extracted dump is automated.)
- Phase 7 Day 97: a signal handler the application installs before `InstallCrashHandler` is replaced by the library's and never runs (observed; the guide says to install after and chain).
- Phase 7 Day 98: `find-core.sh` handles `systemd-coredump` and file-name patterns; cores handed to any other collector are reported as not found. The end-to-end test needs `gdb` and a core the system keeps, and skips (printing why) otherwise or in the sanitizer flavors. Each plain run of that test stores a ~1 MB core in `systemd-coredump` (it ages out by itself), and every crash test on a `systemd-coredump` machine leaves a journal entry with no core.
- Phase 7 Day 99 (2026-11-06): the Breakpad symbol path was not exercised because `dump_syms` and `minidump-stackwalk` are not installed here. Not yet checked by a human: `dump_syms -n rhi_demo build/game/rhi_demo/rhi_demo.debug > rhi_demo.sym` (`-n` makes the module name the program's, as in the dump), file it as `symbols/breakpad/rhi_demo/<build-id>/rhi_demo.sym` (the build id on the `.sym` header line without dashes, upper case), create `symbols/breakpad` yourself (the scripts do not), then `scripts/crash-report.sh <envelope> <program> symbols` (it passes `--symbols-path symbols/breakpad` to `minidump-stackwalk`) and check that the trace names the faulting function. The `lldb` path (names through `symbols/.build-id/`) is tested.
- Phase 7 Day 100 (2026-11-06): `rhi_demo_device_loss_trace` and `renderer_demo_device_loss_trace` need a Vulkan device and skip without one; they ran here (AMD Radeon 860M, RADV). The window `backend` crash-state line and the `gpu` and `frame` lines are covered by unit tests of the mechanism (`crash_state_test.cpp`) and by the demo traces, but no automated test crashes a running demo mid-frame to see all three in one report (`--crash-test` fires before the window and device exist).
- Phase 7 Day 100: the new "a fault inside the crash library's own handler gives up instead of waiting on its lock" path in `os/linux/debug/crash_signal.cpp` has no test (it needs a fault injected inside the library); it was written after a hang found when the main thread's signal stack was too small, which is tested.
- Phase 7 Day 100: `Error` log lines carry a stack trace only when `SetLogErrorTraces(true)` is called (the programs do so in Debug builds); every such line captures and resolves a trace, with no rate limit, so a loop that logs errors is slow in Debug.
- Phase 7 Day 101 (2026-11-06): the cross-target syntax checks (`debug_cross_syntax_windows`, `debug_cross_syntax_macos`) cover the neutral debug headers, `log/log.h`, `log/assert.h` and the platform-free sources (`crash_report`, `thread_info`, `crash_reason`, `crash_state`, `crash_context`, `program_setup`, `death_test`). They do **not** cover `stack_trace_backtrace.cpp` and `crash_handler_sentry.cpp` (libbacktrace's and sentry's headers pull in the target operating system's headers, which are not installed here), `module_list.cpp` and the Linux files. Nothing has been compiled for Windows or macOS with a real SDK. Update, Phase 21 Day 290: `stack_trace_backtrace.cpp` and the neutral debug sources are built for real on macOS (x86-64, Apple clang, three flavors); `crash_handler_sentry.cpp` follows on Day 291.
- Phase 7 Day 101: the audit of Windows and macOS support is read from the vendored sources and the libraries' own support tables; nothing was built or run on either platform. The Windows `on_crash` context and the macOS user context are taken from `sentry_backend_breakpad.cpp`.
- Phase 7 Day 101: the cross-target syntax checks skip (exit 0, "clang++ not found") on a machine without `clang++`, and they are a parse check against stand-in standard headers that are looser than the real libraries, not a build. With only `g++` installed, the guarantee that the neutral code has no Linux-only dependency is not checked at all.
- Phase 7 Day 102 (2026-11-06): a planted `malloc` in `OnCrashEvent` (the hook that writes the report in a crashing program) is caught by `signal_safety_check` only; no allocation-count test runs `OnCrashEvent` itself (the count test covers `WriteCrashReport`). The other five planted regressions were caught by the tests named in the day file.
- Phase 7 Day 102: the closing full run skipped 9 tests by design: the stripped-binary trace test in the normal entries (it runs in its own ctest entry with the env var), the core-dump and throughput tests in the sanitizer flavors, `Building and compiling a graph allocates nothing` under TSan, and the X11 keyboard-layout test (needs `ENGINE_X11_TEST_LAYOUT`).
- Phase 8 Day 106 (2026-11-06): not done or not checked: descriptor-pool counts in `Device::Resources` (only object counts and device memory bytes); the pipeline cache being saved on device destruction was not newly verified (existing `ShutdownPipelines` code); `DeviceHost` with a window (the swapchain branches of `Create`/`Recreate`) has no test until Day 107; Vulkan stale-handle rejection is checked by generation bits only (the rejection is an assert that cannot be unwound through the device lock); no test of the 2^20 slot-reuse wrap of the handle epoch; the `Recreate`-in-a-frame abort is tested with a throwing assert handler, not a death-test child.
- Phase 8 Day 107 (2026-11-06): HDR was not seen on a real HDR display: `HdrMode::ScRgbLinear` is chosen only if the surface offers R16G16B16A16_SFLOAT in extended-sRGB-linear and was exercised by the pure policy tests and the null backend only; `HdrMode::Hdr10` is never granted (the RHI has no 10-bit `Format`). Fullscreen was toggled on this machine's Wayland and X11 sessions only; a compositor that refuses is skipped with a message, not tested; `X11Window::SetFullscreen` before the first map is silently dropped by window managers and the atoms are not cached. Windows and macOS fullscreen are checklists only.
- Phase 8 Day 108 (2026-11-06): the renderer demo's materials hold raw texture handles (`MaterialSystem::SetTexture`), so a demo scene created with `SceneOptions::resources` rebuilds its textures and meshes after a reset but its materials still point at the old handles until Day 109. `game/rhi_demo`'s cube texture is a raw handle and is not registered. The regression tests for the Day 108 review fixes were written after the fixes; they were not run against the unfixed code. (Day 109: the demo's materials now bind textures by owner, so the first part of this entry is fixed.)
- Phase 8 Day 110 (2026-11-06): no test exercises `ApplySettings` with scheduler workers under TSan; a swapchain-class rollback after a lost swapchain is untested on the null backend.
- Phase 8 Day 111 (2026-11-06): no timeout guard around `vkDeviceWaitIdle` on a lost device (a driver that hangs there instead of returning `VK_ERROR_DEVICE_LOST` would freeze the recovery); `fail_allocations_from` during a Vulkan recovery is untested; an injected present loss is exercised on the null backend only. The recovery was tested with losses injected on a healthy device (AMD Radeon 860M, RADV, in the runs of 2026-11-06), never with a real GPU reset or removal.
- Phase 8 Day 112 (2026-11-06): the cross-adapter golden comparison (discrete against integrated GPU) and switching adapters on real hardware have not been run (one GPU here); no test resets with worker jobs queued (`ApplySettings` is owner-thread only by contract); the 50-frame reset sequence compares counts and one material's parameters, not every transform and light by value; removing `DeviceHost`'s `Quiesce` calls is not detected by any test because the device destructor waits for idle itself (an equivalent mutant). The 100-reset Vulkan loop ran 100 times plain and 20 times under ASan on one machine (AMD Radeon 860M, RADV, here: +520 KiB resident, 0 descriptors).
- **Settings system, what Phase 11 left (2026-11-07, Day 138):** (1) remote and live-reloaded configuration (a file watcher, a network store): `ConfigStore::SetChangeListener` exists and the memory store fires it, but no file store watches its file; (2) a UI binding layer: the registry carries labels, groups, ranges, descriptions and `kRestartRequired`/`kAdvanced`/`kHidden` flags for Phase 15's settings screens, nothing consumes them yet; (3) secrets and per-machine state (window positions, the last GPU) are not settings (`kSecret` only keeps a value out of the crash report and listings); (4) the F keys of `renderer_demo` now call `Settings::Apply`, but, as before, they were exercised by script and not with real key presses; (5) `log::LoadConfig` is unchanged (still reads `<Channel>.verbosity` lines); the `log.<channel>.*` settings cover the channels that exist when the registry is built and a program uses one or the other. (6) the file store, the in-memory stores and, since Phase 13 Day 157, the Windows registry store exist: the `NSUserDefaults`, `SharedPreferences` and console save-data stores are checklist entries in `engine/foundation/settings/BACKEND.md` for Phases 21, 31 and 32; (7) one autosave policy (quiet period with backoff); no timer-driven or per-category policies; (8) list-valued settings were cut: a setting holds one bool, integer, float, enum or text value; (9) no migration tooling beyond `MigrationSet` (no offline converter for files); (10) the bootstrap variables in `tools/config_access_check/allowlist.txt` (`ENGINE_JOB_THREADS`, `ENGINE_WINDOW_BACKEND`, the Vulkan loader and validation switches, `ENGINE_CRASH_HANDLER`) are read before any `Settings` exists and are not settings; only `ENGINE_WINDOW_BACKEND`, `ENGINE_RHI_GPU`, `ENGINE_RHI_CACHE_DIR` and `ENGINE_RHI_VALIDATION_SYNC` have a setting twin. (11) a text setting's stored form (quoted, escapes counted) must fit 511 bytes, so 300 backslashes are refused although the unescaped text is short (`TooLong` at entry, not at save); a transport larger than `Value` would lift it; (14) a legacy demo flag (`--no-vsync`, `--validation`) sets the running value and the user file, but `--list-settings` may still name a `--settings` file or the environment as the source when one of them also sets that key.
- Phase 8 Day 113 (2026-11-06): **not looked at by a human:** the live keys F1-F10 of `renderer_demo` and the visual quality of a reset were exercised by script (`--script`, ctests `renderer_demo_script_wayland|x11`, validation silent) and by logs, not with real key presses and eyes. To check by hand: run `renderer_demo`, press each key and look for (1) the window never going black or showing garbage on F1/F5/F6 (a swapchain change), (2) a short freeze of about 40 ms on F2/F3/F9, then the same picture, (3) F5 entering and leaving fullscreen on your compositor, (4) F10 recovering with the same picture after a moment, and the log line `recovered after 1 attempt(s)`. `rhi_demo` got `--gpu` and `--list-gpus` only: it does not use the renderer, so it has no live settings keys. Pipeline-rebuild times were measured with a warm driver shader cache (Mesa's disk cache persists between runs), so the "cold pipeline" cost of eager versus lazy rebuild is not known; the default stays lazy. `renderer_demo --script` accepts 512 steps and 4096 characters. **Looked at by the user on Wayland (2026-11-11, Linux machine, RTX 3060, Vulkan):** orbit, fly, F1, F5, F9, F10 and F11 looked right and the window never went black or showed garbage. **One observation:** pressing F6 (swapchain format: auto, srgb8, rgba16f) made the picture a little darker at one of the steps; which format was not noted. Not investigated (a linear 16-bit format shown without the sRGB encoding would look darker; unconfirmed). **On X11 (through Xwayland) F6 did not darken the picture and everything else worked**, so the darkening is specific to the Wayland surface (a compositor colour-space choice for a 16-bit format is one possibility; unconfirmed).
- Phase 8 Day 114 (2026-11-06): HDR (`ScRgbLinear`) has not been seen on a real HDR display and `Hdr10` is never granted (no 10-bit RHI format); fullscreen was toggled on this machine's compositors only and a compositor that refuses it is skipped by the test; the DXIL and Metal shader paths are untouched by Phase 8. Eager versus lazy pipeline rebuild stays lazy (measurements on one machine; see the Day 113 file).
- **File layer (Phase 9, closed 2026-11-06): deferred on purpose.**
  - Memory-mapped files (a `Map` capability for the asset system's large read-only archives): not built; decided with Phase 22's design.
  - File watching (hot reload of config and shaders): not built; Phase 11 did not build it (see the settings entry above, item 1).
  - Locks (`flock`, `LockFileEx`): only exclusive create and atomic replace exist. Share modes were settled in Phase 13: Windows opens every file
    with `FILE_SHARE_READ | FILE_SHARE_WRITE | FILE_SHARE_DELETE`, so files behave as on POSIX (`io/windows/local_file.cpp`).
  - The mobile and console backends (`engine/foundation/io/BACKEND.md`): the future mobile and console ports (Windows is done, Phase 13; macOS, Phase 21 Days 287-288).
  - Network and pak `File` backends: only the test double `tests/io/slow_file.h` exists; Phase 22 builds the real ones.
  - Windows path comparison folds ASCII case only; non-ASCII names compare exactly (documented in `Path`).
  - Checked on Phase 21 Day 287: `MatchesWildcard`, `ListDirectory`, `WalkDirectory` and `ErrorName` are neutral now (`io/directory.cpp`, `io/error.cpp`; `WalkDirectory` identifies directories through `detail::GetIdentity`). `Exists`/`IsFile`/`IsDirectory`, which only call `Stat`, still live in a backend file: `io/posix/file_system.cpp` (Linux and macOS) and Windows' own.
  - `os::Open`, `os::CreateFileForWriting` and raw `readlink` remain for the assert-path debugger check, `InitCrashContext` (which the assert handler can call lazily) and the crash report; `io::File::Open` allocates and so cannot run there.
  - `tests/io` leaves the temporary directory of an interrupted run behind (`/tmp/re_io_test.*`); nothing sweeps them.




- Phase 11 Day 131 (2026-11-07): **death tests not written.** These `ENGINE_ASSERT` preconditions (the `Aborts:` contracts) have no test that proves the abort; deleting any of them fails nothing. `settings_tests` and `types_tests` are linked with `Catch2::Catch2WithMain` and do not call `debug::RunDeathTestChildIfRequested`, which the death-test helper (`engine/foundation/debug/death_test.h`, support code in `tests/debug`) needs in the program's start-up. Leads for whoever writes them (not tried): the helper above, or the throwing assert handler that `tests/foundation/container_vector_test.cpp`, `tests/debug/assert_family_test.cpp` and `tests/rhi/device_host_test.cpp` already use (Phase 8 Day 106 tested a `Recreate` abort that way).

- All phases (2026-11-07): **838 of the 847 `ENGINE_ASSERT`/`ENGINE_VERIFY`/`ENGINE_CHECK_SLOW` sites under `engine/` have no test in the script's table of covered sites (regenerated 2026-11-20)**; every site, grouped by file with its line and message, is in `KNOWN-ISSUES-untested-aborts.md` (generated by `scripts/list-aborts.py`, whose table of covered sites is edited by hand when a test is added; rerun it and commit the result). The Day 131 entry above is a subset of this list.

- Phase 13 Day 161, observed on Windows (AMD Radeon 860M, driver 26.10.1): after a device loss or a device reset the process holds 0 to 4 more
  blocks of 64 MiB (the Vulkan allocator's block size) than before, while the new device's own device memory (`ResourceCounts::device_memory_bytes`)
  equals the first device's; they are mostly given back one or two losses later, once 128 MiB stayed for 16 losses. Seen in the working set and in
  private bytes alike, over 24 and 30 losses and over 100 resets (788 or 920 MiB, 576 MiB of device memory). Cause not investigated. The memory
  checks of `device_loss_recovery_test.cpp` and `vulkan_reset_golden_test.cpp` allow four such blocks on Windows, so a host-memory leak below about
  25 MiB per loss (or 2.8 MiB per reset) is caught only by the Linux run (code review, declined).
