- Phase 13 Day 161, known limitation: on Windows a program built in Debug that calls `abort()` without the crash handler installed (`--crash-handler=off`,
  or a guide sample run by `docgen samples`) still shows the C runtime's "abort() has been called" dialog; a sample that does so is killed at its
  run timeout (10 s). With the crash handler installed the dialog is off (Day 161). Day 172: the test binaries too: a failing `ENGINE_ASSERT` in
  `rhi_tests.exe` (a mutant run of a new test) waited on the "Microsoft Visual C++ Runtime Library" box until the process was killed, so under
  ctest such a failure costs the test's timeout; only death-test children turn the dialog off (`PlatformPrepareChild`).
- Phase 13 Day 160: `vulkan_pipeline_test.cpp`'s "the cache directory cannot be written" case does not run on Windows (a directory's
  read-only attribute does not stop writes, as for the settings tests of Day 157).
- Phase 13 Day 157, known limitation: on Windows a file has no mode bits, so a settings or session file the engine writes "private" (0600 on Linux) takes its
  directory's ACL (`io/BACKEND.md`, `CreateTempFile`): private in the user's own folders, readable by others in a shared folder. The 0600 checks of
  `file_store_test.cpp` and `engine_settings_test.cpp` run on Linux only.
- Phase 13 Day 157: two settings tests that need a directory refusing writes (`Save: a read-only directory is a status...`, `Save with the command line's
  values...`) and the read-only-directory half of a `FileConfigStore` test skip on Windows, where a directory's read-only attribute does not stop writes;
  an ACL-based variant is not built.
- Phase 13 Day 156, check not done: a Windows minidump has not been opened in WinDbg or cdb with the stored symbols by a person; neither debugger is
  installed on the Windows PC (`scripts/open-dump.ps1` exits 66 there). The dumps were read back with `crash_extract --modules` and their frames
  named through DbgHelp from the store (`crash_report_script_windows_test.cpp`), not by a debugger.
- Phase 13 Day 156, not tested: `EnablePostMortemDumps` reporting `Enabled` on Windows, and WER writing a local dump of an engine program: the PC has
  no `HKLM\...\Windows Error Reporting\LocalDumps` key and setting one needs an administrator. Measured instead: WER ignores the same values under
  HKCU on this Windows 11 build (Windows 10.0.26300).
- Phase 13 Day 155, known limitation (not a failure): a fast fail (`__fastfail`, `/GS` stack-cookie failures, some C runtime checks) ends a Windows
  process in the kernel with `0xC0000409`; no handler sees it, so it leaves no engine report and no dump in the crash handler's directory
  (`crash_end_to_end_windows_test.cpp` checks this). Windows Error Reporting LocalDumps, where an administrator set them up, is the only dump it
  can leave.
- Phase 13 Day 155, known limitation: on Windows the crash report opens its report file at crash time with `CreateFileW`, which converts the path
  with a heap allocation inside Windows; a crash while another thread holds the process heap's lock could stop the report there (the dump is
  written before the report). The allocation check sees only the C runtime's allocations. Not observed; from the day's design review.
- Phase 13 Day 155: the Windows allocation check of the crash report (`crash_alloc_windows_test.cpp`, the debug C runtime's `_CrtSetAllocHook`) runs
  only in the plain Debug flavor; it skips under AddressSanitizer, whose allocator the hook does not see.
- Phase 13 Day 155: on Windows the crash hook remembers at most 256 threads registered with `RegisterThreadForCrashHandling` (their stack ranges, for
  an `abort()` on them); entries are not removed when a thread ends, so after 256 registrations an `abort()` on a newer thread is reported
  without its id and name. A matched entry of an ended thread whose stack was reused gives the wrong id (its name read is guarded).
- Phase 13 Day 154: on Windows an assertion's trace is resolved through DbgHelp, which allocates from the process heap; an assert that fires while
  the heap lock is held (inside a CRT allocation) could deadlock while printing it. Linux resolves without `malloc`. Not observed; from review.
- Phase 13 Day 154: under MSVC's Debug build the inlined-frame case of `stack_trace_test` skips (MSVC inlines nothing at `/Od`); the clang-cl
  build runs and passes it.
- Phase 13 Day 153: the Windows CPU list on a machine with several processor groups is tested only with recorded data (this PC has one group);
  whether `GetProcessGroupAffinity` lists every group a Windows 11 process may use there is unverified.
- Phase 13 Days 151-152: not checked on the Windows PC: every symbolic-link case of `tests/io` (Developer Mode is off, so they skip with the reason;
  directory links fall back to junctions where a test only needs one), and the long-path behavior with `LongPathsEnabled = 1` (the setting is off;
  the tests pass with it off).
- Phase 13 Day 174 (2026-11-08, Linux): `renderer_demo --rhi dx12` and `rhi_demo --rhi dx12` on Linux exit 1 with "the dx12 backend is not registered in this program (link rhi_backends and call rhi::RegisterCompiledBackends ...)". The program does link `rhi_backends`; the backend is not built on Linux. The refusal is correct, its reason is misleading.

- **Phase 27 Day 449 (2026-11-18, Linux): limits of the budgets and the sandbox** (the Day 444 findings are fixed: budgeted states run without the JIT, `pcall`, `xpcall` and the coroutine functions re-raise a tripped budget, the pattern functions are step-limited, the machine code of a trusted state is bounded by LuaJIT's own `maxmcode` default of 512 KB). (1) An instruction budget is counted in blocks of 1000 instructions, and the time budget is checked at the same points, so a call overshoots by up to one block; a long-running C function (`table.sort` of a huge array, `string.rep`, `string.format`) is not interrupted, only bounded by the memory cap and the size of its data. (2) A trusted state (JIT on) has no instruction or time budget at all, including the top level of a loaded chunk; only the memory cap applies. (3) The policy after a tripped budget (`script.on_budget`: reset, disable, log) is the world's, Day 452; the backend only returns `BudgetExceeded` and leaves the state usable. (4) Scripts can shadow a global name in their own environment; the engine's own environment and the library tables are read-only views, but a view hides `pairs` over the library (`pairs(string)` gives nothing).
- **Phase 27 Day 444: ThreadSanitizer reports 3 data races inside `libgcc_s.so`'s unwinder** when 8 threads throw and catch Lua errors (heap blocks the unwinder allocates itself; none in LuaJIT's C or our code); the engine's tsan flavor will need a suppression with the first multithreaded test (Day 451). The sanitizer runs link a plain LuaJIT, so its assembly core is not instrumented.
- **Phase 27 Day 444: LuaJIT on the other platforms** (the spec's section 8 is from reading the tree): built with MSVC and `clang-cl` on Day 473 and on macOS on Day 474 (x86-64, run; arm64, compile-only). `LUAJIT_DISABLE_JIT` (`ENGINE_LUAJIT_JIT=OFF`) was built and run on Linux and, with MSVC, on Windows (Day 473), and on the Mac (2026-11-19: the script test programs only, plain).

- **Phase 27 Day 445 (2026-11-18, Linux): the sanitizer flavors of `script_tests` link the same uninstrumented `luajit` library.** AddressSanitizer sees LuaJIT's C code only through the allocator the engine gives it (and its own `mmap`ed machine code and `lj_vm.S` not at all); ThreadSanitizer sees the engine's side of every call (the state ownership checks, the buffers) but not the memory accesses of LuaJIT's assembly core or of generated code. LuaJIT's error unwinding through `libgcc_s` is reported by TSan (see the Day 444 entry); a suppression is added with the first multithreaded test (Day 451). `third_party/luajit` builds on Linux and Windows x86-64 (Windows from Day 473, where the asan flavor likewise links the plain library); macOS: Day 474.

## Phase 15 (Day 185)

- The Wayland pointer, wheel, focus and key handlers (`wayland_input.cpp`) have no automated test: no test-compositor seam exists, and the real compositor cannot be made to send input. Only open + Resize is checked. Key logic is tested through `KeyboardEvents`; X11 is tested with injected events.
- No IME composition; dead keys and compose depend on the locale's Compose file (without one they are not combined).
- (Day 186) Cursor image, cursor shapes and the pointer lock were seen by the user with `input_probe` on Wayland and X11 (2026-11-11) and work; the X11 and Wayland difference in hiding the pointer during a lock is filed above.
- (Day 186) Wayland: a compositor without `wp_cursor_shape_v1` gets no standard shapes (`libwayland-cursor` fallback not used); one without the pointer constraint protocols gets no lock. X11 shows cursor images at pixel size (no scaling).
- (Day 187) Wayland clipboard transfers (offer, `send`, `receive`, cancelled, primary selection) have no end-to-end test: no test compositor, and `set_selection` needs the serial of a real input event. Only the listener table, the `Missing` path and the local answer are tested; the pipe machinery has its own tests.
- (Day 187) The X11 clipboard tests share the real desktop selection: Klipper takes a selection over moments after it is set and may refuse `TARGETS`, so those assertions are made right after the set or accept the announced takeover. Seen while writing the tests (not a product bug).
- (Day 187) `STRING` (Latin-1) is served only when the text is lossless; the X11 error scope that protects the answers is process-global (not safe against another thread's Xlib errors).
- (Day 187) `X11Window::Create opens a mapped window on the real X server` and `X11Window takes the pointer grab on request, releases it, and releases it when destroyed` (phase 1) fail while the session is locked (`kscreenlocker_greet` running) even with the logind query disabled; both fail the same on the Day 186 commit (clean worktree build, 2026-11-10).
- (Day 188) Wayland drag and drop has no end-to-end test (no test compositor; `start_drag` needs the serial of a real button press): only the listener table is tested. The X11 source-side tests skip while the session is locked, because the screen locker's window is under the pointer instead of the scripted target, so XDND sending is verified against a real target only when run on an unlocked desktop.
- (Day 188) The X11 drag image is not drawn (`DragSource::image` is ignored on X11); drag data is copied at `BeginDrag` (no lazy callback).
- (Day 189) A bordered rounded rect is two quads (fill and ring) until the vertex carries a second colour; the allocation-free claim is checked by array capacities, not by an allocation counter.
- (Day 205, Windows PC) Not seen by eye on Windows: mouse look in the viewport (the pointer lock), the cursor shapes on hover, menus near a screen edge, typing AltGr characters and a dead key into a text field, resizing by the frame while the UI keeps drawing (the modal-loop callback), a drag to and from Explorer, and copy and paste with another program. The tests post Windows' messages to the window and exchange data between windows of the test process; no real input was injected. A dead key was not tested: it needs a keyboard layout loaded for the test, a change to the system's settings.
- (Day 205) Windows: the drag image is not drawn (`IDragSourceHelper` is not used); a drag out of the window is modal (`DoDragDrop`), so the frame loop pauses and only the modal-loop callback runs. `ContentScale` is 1 on X11 and Wayland: a high-DPI X11 screen gets small widgets and Wayland scales the window's buffer (no `wl_surface` buffer scale yet). The OLE objects (`win32_dragdrop.h`) count references but free nothing and live until the window closes: a drop target in another program that kept the data object after `DoDragDrop` returned would call into freed memory after the close (a reference left at close is logged; none was seen). Declined in the Day 205 review: self-deleting COM objects need a raw `new`, which `raw_new_check` forbids.
- (Phase 16 Days 217-218) The six lldb-dependent debug tests were not run after their port (lldb is not installed on the Linux machine); they compiled. `rhi_tests_tsan` run as one whole binary aborted with a TSan internal CHECK in a test-port agent's run (not checked against the commit before); under ctest the tsan flavor passes.
- (Phase 16, Day 214) `WFormatTo` and `ScanFrom` carry no format-attribute check (templates), unlike `FormatTo`.

- Phase 17 Day 232 (2026-11-10): `ecs::SetParent`, `ClearParent` and `DestroyRecursive` collect every `Parent` in the world on each call (O(world) per call), so building a large hierarchy through `SetParent` is quadratic; creating children with `Parent` and `HierarchyDepth` directly is linear. Not measured; the Day 235 benchmark builds a 100k-entity hierarchy through it without trouble; still not measured on its own (a child index if it matters).
- Phase 18 Day 239 (2026-11-10), deferred: the static BVH builds on one thread; the parallel build of the spatial spec (section 6: the recursion split over the job scheduler above a size threshold, partitions in sequential order so the tree equals the sequential one byte for byte) is not written. The single-threaded build of 100,000 boxes takes 39 to 41 ms in Release on the Windows PC (target under 50 ms). For Day 245 or Day 248 to take up if a scene load needs it.
- Phase 17 Day 234 (2026-11-10): `GBufferPass::Prepare` has no overload taking a precomputed visible list. Measured on the Linux machine (Release): culling 100k objects is 0.57 ms on one thread and 0.08 ms chunk-parallel with 8 workers, too small a part of a frame to justify a second path; a visible-list overload of `RenderScene::Cull` (planned for Phase 18, not built) would revisit it.
- Phase 17 Day 235 (2026-11-10, Linux machine, Release, Ryzen 9 7950X3D): two ECS scenarios are slower than the object-oriented baseline's equivalent, outside the day's pass conditions (iteration 5.6x faster, 85 percent efficiency at 4 workers): structural churn (10 percent created and destroyed plus 10 percent tag toggles per frame, 100k entities) takes 1.58 ms against 0.65 ms, and hierarchy propagation (chains of five, every root moved, sequential) takes 3.2 ms against 2.2 ms. Not profiled. `Changed<T>` with 1 percent random writes is 1.2x faster than the baseline (chunk precision). Numbers in `docs/guide/ecs.md` "Measured cost". Parallel efficiency falls to 48 percent at 32 workers on 16 cores / 32 threads.
- Phase 17 Day 235 (2026-11-10, Linux machine, load average 13): `An entity-built scene renders the same pixels as the hand-built RenderScene [tsan]` (`tests/scene/scene_render_test.cpp`, phase 17) aborted five runs in a row with `ThreadSanitizer: CHECK failed: tsan_interceptors_posix.cpp:2206 "((thr->slot)) != (0)"` on the default (hardware) Vulkan adapter, and skips with `ENGINE_RHI_GPU=llvmpipe`. The same test passed in the tsan run of Day 234 (`ran 1268 of 5454`), its plain and asan variants pass, and no file of the test or of its libraries changed since. Not investigated.
- Phase 17 end-of-phase review (2026-11-10, Linux machine), findings declined or deferred, each with its reason (the review had 4 reviewers and found 1 bug, fixed):
  - Limits of misuse, documented, not fixed: `Query::Finish` calls `World::Advance` (a plain increment) and races a `Tick()` read of another system in the same wave (today only `render_sync` and the camera system share a wave and neither reads the tick); two `ForEachChunkParallel` calls in one system apply their commands interleaved by range, so their order depends on the worker count; declared access is counted (`access_violations`), not enforced; `RangesFor(chunks, 0)` cannot size a pool because grain 0 resolves to 4 ranges per participant; a `CommandBuffer` applied repeatedly without `Clear` keeps growing its placeholder list, and a placeholder is not tied to its buffer; `Changed<T>` or `Added<T>` together with `Without<T>` compiles and matches nothing; a raw `Set<Parent>` that bypasses `SetParent` can put a parent and child in one depth bucket for a frame; `Decompose` does not handle a mirrored matrix; children of a parent that has only a `WorldTransform` are not re-propagated when that is written.
  - Scene sync: reusing a schedule with a new `World` leaves the old world's objects in the `RenderScene`; an entity that swaps between mesh and light after its handle exists is not re-created; the sync must run every frame (removal log entries live two frames).
  - Hardening not done: `EntityTable::Free` pushes onto the free list after clearing the slot (an out-of-memory abort would leak the slot); a refused archetype layout is not cached; `Validate` does not check the free list, stamps or removal logs; `ComponentRegistry::Info` checks its id only with `ENGINE_ASSERT`; `~World` destroys resources before components.
- Phase 18 Day 245 (2026-11-10, Linux machine, Release): the sync misses the spec's pass conditions for moving objects. 100,000 entities in two sets (dynamic BVH, a walk of 1.5 units for boxes of 1 unit): a frame with 1 percent moving 7.6 ms, 10 percent 73 ms, 100 percent 740 ms; `ApplyMoves` is most of it (3.6 to 7 microseconds per moved item and set). Spec targets: 1.0 microsecond per moved object at 1 percent, all-moving agents in 80 ms. Day 248 measures the grid and tuning; not investigated further.
- Phase 18 Day 245 (2026-11-10), deferred: the static BVH build is single-threaded (also Day 239's entry); the sync does not build static trees in parallel.
- Phase 18 open items filed for later: per-tile light lists from the tree, GPU-side culling, streaming of world cells (Phase 33), a parallel static-tree build, serialization of trees (rebuilt on load). `plans/phase-25-physics.md` already names Phase 18 as its broad phase.
- Phase 18 end-of-phase review, declined or deferred (each with its reason; none fires in the engine's own use): hash grid cells are never freed and the pair walk scans every cell ever used; the grid's oversized-item pair loop is cubic in the oversized count (a few thousand large static items would be slow); `SpatialSet::RemovePending` is linear (removing n pending statics before a rebuild is quadratic); any single static add or removal rebuilds the whole static tree that frame (no threshold); the dynamic tree's depth is not proven below the 64-entry traversal stack (a subtree that does not fit is skipped and counted in `TraversalStats::refused`, which `QueryStats` does not carry); the sync's destroyed-entity cleanup relies on the world's two-frame removal log, so the sync system must run every frame; `PairTracker::largest_margin_` only grows and statics must be touched when added; `PairTrackerConfig::meets` is public and must be kept symmetric (use `SetMeets`); `items_tested` means different things per structure; `DebugLines` draws a grid's items as nodes; `SpatialWorld::Clear` on a moved-from world; `left` does not count a membership of zero; the 21-bit grid cell sentinels alias real cells beyond 2^20 cells; `TransformAabb` of an unbounded box gives NaN; a static entity whose transform changes keeps its old static box and its refused moves are counted.
- Phase 18 spatial pass conditions missed or open (Day 248 measure, spec section 4.1): the 40-unit overlap on a static tree at 100,000 items takes 2.7 us (target 2 us; result-bound, about 200 hits) and a timed quiet-scene sync was not measured; and
- Phase 18 Day 248, spatial pass condition missed (Release, Ryzen 9 7950X3D, Linux): 100,000 all-moving agents through the ECS sync take about 175 ms on one thread (target 80 ms with the pair update, 55-63 ms more); the change after Day 249 runs the moves of each set as a job, so 8 workers take 93 ms (1.9x, target 2x). The dynamic tree re-inserts about 0.85 us per move in each of two sets; the remaining options are a grid for the physics set and a margin of 0.5. Options and numbers in the design spec section 4.1; to be decided with the user at the phase close. On the Windows PC (2026-11-11, Release, Ryzen AI 7 350) the same benchmark took 259 to 264 ms with 1, 2, 4 and 8 workers alike; after the commit that runs each set's moves as its own job (6ba686c7), 240 ms with 1 worker and 131 to 132 ms with 2, 4 and 8 (two runs).
- Phase 18 Day 248 deferred: the visible-list overload of `RenderScene::Cull` and a parallel `CullFrustum`; the rendered `spatial_demo` (window, line pass, golden image, README gate) needs a renderer line pass and an ECS-driven scene that no phase plans yet (the user decides where it belongs); the `renderer.culling` setting; light culling through the tree; a parallel static-tree build.
- Phase 18 Day 247 deferred: light culling through the tree and per-tile light lists (lights stay a flat list), a parallel cull over top-level subtrees; the `renderer.culling` setting moves to Day 248.
- Phase 18 Day 246 (2026-11-10, Linux machine, plain): X11 window tests failed in whole-plain `--affected` runs, a different pair each run (`X11Window takes the pointer grab on request...`, `X11Window::Create opens a mapped window on the real X server`, `X11Window releases keys and modifiers on a real focus loss...`, `X11Window maps event state bits to engine modifiers`); not investigated (the Day 237 entry is the same family).
- Phase 19 Day 254 (2026-11-11, Linux machine): a database file is not protected against a second process. The file layer has no file locks, and a lock file would stay behind after a crash; SQLite's locks and the write-ahead-log index live in this process. Options: a lock operation on `io::File` (`flock` / `LockFileEx` in the backends), or a lock file with a liveness check. Until then a database belongs to one process (documented in `docs/guide/database.md`).
- Phase 19 Day 255 (2026-11-11, Linux machine): The tsan suppression (`tests/database/tsan.supp`) is Linux only. Benchmark taken on one machine only (20,000 rows; no scan or `:memory:` figures).
- Phase 19 Day 258 (2026-11-11, Linux machine): Limits filed: the pending-reference list for `EntityRef` fields of user components and the Merge remapping are Day 260's loader; `KeyIndex::Sync` advances the world tick (`Query::Finish`), so it belongs at a sync point.
- Phase 19 Day 259 (2026-11-11, Linux machine): Limits filed: `MemoryStore` copies the whole committed set at `Begin` (correct and simple, not fast); `Store::Put` has no record-size limit in `MemoryStore` (the SQL store applies `max_record_bytes`).
- Phase 19 Day 260 (2026-11-11, Linux machine): limits filed. (a) A load checks `ecs::Parent` targets against the load and the world, but not the `EntityRef` fields of user components: they keep their key and a dangling one is not reported (the field descriptor has no way to hand the key out). (b) The spatial test after a load compares item counts and `WorldBounds` after running the sync system, not a brute-force rebuild of the sets. (c) A failure of the apply pass that is not bad data (the world out of memory, a component limit) destroys the entities that load created but cannot restore what `Replace` destroyed or what `Merge` changed. (d) `Selection::Filter` is a save-only selection.
- Phase 19 Day 261 (2026-11-11, Linux machine): limits filed. (a) An incremental save does not rewrite a child's record when only its parent gains or loses a key (the child's chunk is unchanged); `SaveSession::Reset` fixes it. (b) A session follows the whole world (`AllKeyed` only). (c) An idle incremental save at 100,000 entities took 18 ms, mostly fixed per-save work; not investigated (pass condition is Day 265).
- Phase 19 Day 262 (2026-11-11, Linux machine): limit filed. `SqlStore` uses one connection, so while a batch is open every call from another thread is `Busy` (MemoryStore lets readers see the committed state).
- Phase 19 Day 263 (2026-11-11, Linux machine): limits filed. (a) A `u64` field is a signed `INTEGER` column (its bit pattern), so range queries above 2^63 order wrongly; a NaN is NULL and matches no comparison. (b) Only scalar and vector fields have columns; strings, arrays, matrices, boxes and references are found only by loading. (c) Column tables follow appended fields in place; other changes (a removed or reordered field) rebuild the table. (d) The `storage.layout` setting arrived on Day 264.
- Phase 19 Days 251-263 on the Windows PC (2026-11-11): three shared changes were built and tested on the Windows PC; the Linux machine has since built them with Clang and GCC (Day 264) and only the Mac's `test_phase_script` run is open: `engine/storage/component_registry.h` (the `fields` and `upgrades` defaults are `InitializerList<T>()`, not `{}`: MSVC C2797), `engine/storage/field.h` (the enum branch of `detail::EncodeValue` no longer returns: MSVC C4702 unreachable code), `tests/test_phase_script/CMakeLists.txt` (`affected_side_phase_wrong_flavor` uses `--flavor asan`; Windows refuses tsan). Also from the Windows PC, the Phase 19 close: the tutorial sample `docs/tutorials/saving-a-world.md` checks the results of `Autosaver::Update` and `Flush` (MSVC C4834), open until the Linux machine runs `ctest -L docs`.
- 2026-11-12 (Windows PC): after the reformatting and clang-tidy commits, three things broke the Windows build and were fixed there; built and tested on the Windows PC only, open until the Linux machine builds them with Clang and GCC: (1) `engine/storage/component_registry.h`: the `InitializerList<T>()` defaults of the Phase 19 fix had been rewritten to `= {}` by the mechanical pass of `bbaaba2c` (MSVC C2797 again; `readability-redundant-member-init` is not enabled, so no check asked for it); restored with a comment. (2) `tests/types/int128_test.cpp`: `modernize-use-nullptr` turned the literal `0` of `(a <=> b) < 0` into `nullptr`, which libstdc++ accepts and the Microsoft library rejects (C2678 with cl, invalid operands with clang-cl); the test compares with `Ordering::less`/`greater`/`equal`. (3) `tests/windows/CMakeLists.txt` (Windows only): the generated `windows_header_check` included the Mac's new `os/posix/debug/posix_debug.h` (`spawn.h` not found); `posix/` is skipped like the other per-platform directories.
- Phase 19 Day 264 (2026-11-11, Linux machine): (b) The storage demo does not compare a render-scene draw list: `MeshRenderer` holds a GPU mesh with no stored form until Phase 22. (c) A save holds the encoded records of the whole selection in memory until the write (each is freed as it is written); measure on Day 265. (d) `SaveAsync` encodes inside the call (on the workers), so the caller waits for the encoding of the changed entities; only the writes are in the background. (e) `SaveAsync` and `Autosaver` must be used from the scheduler's main thread.
- Phase 19 Day 265 (2026-11-11, Linux machine): **pass condition missed:** an incremental save of 1 percent of 1,000,000 entities written at random takes 1.23 s against 0.25 s, because the change stamps are per chunk (a chunk holds about 260 entities, so 1 percent at random touches 93 percent of them and 928,297 entities are encoded; only 9,951 records are written). With the changes in neighbouring entities it takes 128 ms. Options: (a) accept, since games write in clusters; (b) a per-entity dirty bit set by a write hook (cost on every write); (c) a sub-chunk stamp (a stamp per 16 rows, a few percent more memory per chunk); (d) hash only, skipping the encode with a cheaper component-level hash. No decision. On the Windows PC (2026-11-11, Release, Ryzen AI 7 350, two runs) the random case took 1,490-1,498 ms, and the neighbouring case **also missed**: 381-392 ms (1,075 ms with columns), against 128 ms on Linux; the other figures are in the Day 265 file.
- Phase 19 Day 265 (2026-11-11): end-of-phase review findings **filed, not fixed**, storage core:
  - a child is not relinked when its parent's key changes
  - `Load` with `Replace`/`Merge` is not atomic when it fails part-way
  - `KeyIndex` goes stale after two `EndFrame`s without a refresh
  - duplicate keys in a world block saves
  - `Save` does not check the store header
  - one entity that cannot be encoded aborts the whole save
  - a forgotten entity's old key is not removed from the session in an incremental plan
  - pieces are ordered by registration order
  - the upgrade-step output limit is not enforced per step
  - SQL identifiers are not quoted and names differing only in case collide
  - column-layout staleness cases 2-4
  - one bad record blocks `Open` of a column store (the refill fails)
  - `SqlStore::FailAfter` lives in the production class
  - `VerifyColumns` treats -0.0 as equal to 0.0
  - index-name collisions
  - test gaps for `FindKeys` edge cases
- Phase 19 Day 265 (2026-11-11): end-of-phase review findings **filed, not fixed**, database module (the gate, temp-file, sync-mode, `ByteWriter` and documentation findings of the same review were fixed in the triage pass):
  - a second process is not excluded from a WAL database (locks and the WAL index are in process memory only)
  - `VfsOpen` has no read-only fallback, and a read-only open of a WAL database creates an empty `-wal` file
  - the crash model never drops unsynced data, so it proves call order, not durability
  - `PRAGMA journal_mode` may silently not take effect while other connections hold the file
  - two spellings of one path may get separate lock tables
  - `VfsAccess` ignores its flags (an empty `-wal` counts as existing)
  - `Commit`'s "still open on failure" is not true for every SQLite code
  - the TSAN suppressions cover only two functions and SQLite is not instrumented
  - statement-cache lookups are linear and miss text with trailing blanks
  - static-destruction order, and temp files orphaned when the process dies
  - fault-injection state is global
- Phase 20 Day 275 (declined in review): an unreliable fragmented message is reassembled under the key (channel, kind, sequence); if the 16-bit sequence wraps inside `reassembly_timeout_nanos` (more than 13,000 unreliable fragmented messages per second on one channel) with a fragment lost in the first one, a new message with the same totals could complete the stale one with mixed bytes. Reliable kinds are not affected.

- Phase 20 Day 271 (a part kept from the deleted entry): the reason `TcpStream` reports for a kernel connection timeout (`ETIMEDOUT` from keep-alive) has no test: it cannot be provoked on loopback.
- Phase 20 Day 279 (declined in review): the global receive budget of `UdpTransport` (`max_total_receive_buffer_bytes`, 256 MiB) ends the connection that would pass it, not the one holding the most. With the default caps (8 connections per address x 16 MiB each) a few addresses that buffer out-of-order data could fill it and make honest connections the ones that get ended. Fair-share shedding (end the largest holder) and reserving reassembly bytes only as fragments arrive are Day 281's measurements to make, or a later phase's.
- Phase 20 Day 280 (deferred by the user, 2026-11-12): QUIC, TLS and encryption are not built. The UDP and TCP transports do not authenticate peers or protect payloads and are for trusted networks only (`docs/guide/net.md`). Phase 20 closes without them; where the work lands later is the user's call (it would renumber the plan).
- Phase 20 Day 281 (2026-11-12, Linux machine), **not measured**: the pass conditions of the networking spec (section 5) that exercise the reactor and coroutine layer under load (250,000 echo round trips per second at 1,000 connections with four participants; at most 1.0 `epoll_wait` wake-ups per round trip; idle resume p99 under 200 us; saturated hand-off p99 under 5 ms), UDP at 50,000 reliable-ordered and 100,000 unreliable messages per second, the congestion condition (80 % of a 10 Mbit/s, 50 ms link), the codec benchmark, and CPU with 10,000 idle connections. `tests/net/net_bench.cpp` measures what the polled transports do (echo latency, 1,000 chatty simulated connections, simulated stream throughput); the first coroutine user of `net::Reactor` measures the rest.
- Phase 20 Day 281 (2026-11-12), limitations filed: the soak test is 10,000 simulated TCP connections for a few seconds, not 10 simulated minutes of lossy UDP traffic, and checks leaks with asan rather than a memory number; `tools/net_probe resolve` takes literal addresses only (host names need the reactor); `game/net_demo` has no storage `AsyncLoad` over TCP (no network `Store` exists); the mixed-connections TSan test planned on Day 277 is not written; the replication interest-callback slot is not built.
- Phase 20 Day 281 (end-of-phase review, declined or deferred, 2026-11-12): (a) **UDP: two transports that both listen and connect to each other can misroute payload packets**: a payload header has no direction bit and `FindPayloadTarget` searches incoming connections by wire id first, so with A and B both listening and each connecting to the other, the first incoming connection of each has wire id 1 and the other connection's packets reach it (`udp_transport.cpp`, `FindPayloadTarget`); needs a direction bit in the packet header (a format change, Phase 22 or the first peer-to-peer user). Client/server use is not affected. (b) **UDP: a reliable message can run more than 1,024 messages (or 16 MiB, or the fragment cap) ahead of a lost one and the receiver then ends the connection with `ProtocolError`**: the sender limits only the 256 frames in flight; the simulated transport instead withholds the acknowledgement. Needs a receive-window limit in the acknowledgements. (c) **UDP: 256 incomplete unreliable fragmented messages fill the reassembly slots and the next fragmented reliable message ends the connection** (`udp_transport.cpp` where `max_reassemblies` is reached; `return !reliable`); needs eviction of the oldest partial unreliable message. (d) **TCP receive memory is not counted in `net.connection_budget_bytes`** (only the send queues are): up to `max_connections` x `max_message_bytes` of partially received frames. (e) **`TcpStream::SendFrame` fails with `LimitExceeded` for a frame between `send_queue_bytes` (256 KiB) and `max_frame_bytes` (1 MiB)**; set `net.send_queue_bytes` above the largest message. (f) `net_probe echo` ignores a full send queue, so `send --file` of a very large file can time out. (g) `Socket::SetLinger(true, seconds > 0)` blocks `close` on Linux (inside `Reactor::Close`'s lock) and leaks the handle on Windows; the engine passes 0 only, and the `socket.h` contract should refuse a positive value. (h) A wait that ends inside `Reactor::Begin` and whose re-arm of the other direction then fails drops that other waiter's continuation (needs an injected `epoll_ctl` failure to reproduce). (i) `Socket::Connect` while one is under way returns `WouldBlock` on POSIX and `AlreadyConnected` on Windows; `Poller::Arm` returns `Other` for `EPERM` and `ENOSPC` where its contract promises `Invalid`/`LimitExceeded`.
- **Phase 22 Day 314 (2026-11-13, Linux): the writer's out-of-memory reply is untested.** `BlobWriter::AddSection` and `Finish` return `TooLarge` when the allocator fails, but a test cannot run in the sanitizer flavors (their allocators abort on a huge request, found when the first version of the test failed under asan and tsan).
- **Phase 22 Day 315 (2026-11-13, Linux): not tested.** The symbolic-link branches of `FilesystemBackend` (a link out of the root is skipped; a loop fails to resolve) have no test because the file layer has no way to create a link; the walk goes 64 directories deep and a deeper blob is silently not indexed; `HostMemoryBackend::Install` can leave a resource without its paths if the table runs out of memory in the middle of a commit (untested, like the Day 314 writer).
- **Phase 22 Day 315 (2026-11-13, Linux): the filesystem backend holds its lock for a whole rescan.** `Watch` (and the first index build) scan the directory under the backend's mutex, so an `Open` on another thread waits for the walk. Not measured; a copy-on-write index would remove it.
- **Phase 22 Day 316 (2026-11-13, Linux): the 5 ms open bound for 100,000 entries is not measured in a release build.** The hidden `[.][asset-bench]` case printed `open 36.98 ms, lookup 29.6 ns each` in the debug build with an in-memory file (the index read, the validation and the slot table all run unoptimized); Day 330 takes the release figures.
- **Phase 22 Day 316 (2026-11-13, Linux): not tested or declined.** The 1 MiB ranged read is not in the benchmark (only open and lookup); `asset_mint pack` has no test (tests drive the library; only the not-a-directory error was run by hand), reads every blob into memory before writing and knows only the built-in resource types until Day 327; `PakBackend::Open` reads the 64-byte blob header on every call to check the id; the header's base-identity bytes are covered by neither `Open` nor `Verify` (a changed one makes the pak a patch of another base, which `PakBackend` refuses with `MissingDependency`); a `WrongTarget` message is static, so the two tags are in `PakReader::Detail()`.
- **Phase 22 Day 317 (2026-11-13, Linux): the 4-byte attribute alignment, open until the Linux machine and the Mac run it.** The Windows PC (2026-11-13) found that D3D12 refuses an attribute aligned only to its component size: in the new `tests/rhi/dx12_vertex_format_test.cpp` (phase 22), the seven float-class formats at offset 10 (16-bit) or 9 (`SNorm8x4`) each failed `CreateInputLayout` (debug layer: "Input layout for slot 0 is invalid, as there is not a correct alignment for all of its members at any offset"); at offsets 8 and 12 all decode to the expected color. `VertexFormatAlignment` is now 4 for every format and `GraphicsPipelineDesc::Validate` refuses any other offset (`engine/rhi/enums.h`, `desc.cpp`; `types_test.cpp` checks offset 14 for `UNorm16x4` and 13 for `SNorm8x4`; `docs/guide/renderer.md`). Waiting: the Linux machine runs `types_test`, the Vulkan readback and the renderer suites on the changed rule; the Mac runs its suites on it (its Metal twin draws at offset 8, a multiple of 4, so Metal's own answer at offset 10 is not measured; Metal's documentation asks for a multiple of 4). The DX12 and Metal tables are built and their readback twins pass (the Mac's half: Day 317's file).
- **Phase 22 Day 317 (2026-11-13, Linux): not tested.** `UInt16x4` has only the vertex-buffer feature check (`UInt8x4` joint indices are read back by the skinning test of Day 322, `tests/rhi/skinning_gpu_test.cpp`).
- **Phase 22 Day 318 (2026-11-13, Linux): minted skeleton bytes may differ between compilers or architectures.** The inverse bind matrices are computed in `f64` and rounded to `f32`; no `-ffp-contract=off` is set anywhere, so a compiler that fuses multiply-adds (arm64 Clang by default) may round the last bit differently from x86-64 GCC. Not observed; byte-identical minting was only compared between runs on one machine.
- **Phase 22 Day 318 (2026-11-13, Linux): declined or deferred.** `SkeletonView::Open` does not recompute the skeleton hash from the parents and names (a blob can carry a hash that lies; the container hash only shows it was not altered) and does not check that names are unique (loading stays O(joints)); retargeting between skeletons is out of scope; the joint-flags section of the plan is omitted (nothing uses it); `asset_mint remint` and the v0 upgrade test wait for Day 333 (a version-0 blob is refused with the remint message); `asset_mint mint-skeleton` has no test of its own (the library is tested, the command was run by hand).
- **Phase 22 Day 319 (2026-11-13, Linux): planned and not done.** (1) The trusted zero-copy `GpuMesh` entry (a `TrustedMeshView` with one or two vertex streams) and its device-reset test: `asset` does not link `renderer` yet and `GpuMesh` has one vertex buffer; Day 329 (the GPU-backed loaders) builds the GPU object and must add the second stream. (2) A minted cube and sphere are not compared with `MakeCubeMesh` / `MakeSphereMesh` (same link). (3) The minter keeps source vertex and index order (no cache or fetch reordering). (4) Texture coordinates are always `UNorm16x2` with a per-mesh offset and scale (no `Float16x2` choice). (5) `asset_mint remint` for meshes and the golden-fixture upgrade test wait for Day 333; a version-0 blob is refused with the remint message. (6) `MeshView::Open` does not scan indices or the skin stream (`CheckIndices`, `CheckSkin` do); `asset_mint mint-mesh` has no test of its own.
- **Phase 22 Day 319 (2026-11-13, Linux): out of scope for v1, filed for Phase 31.** Quantized positions (a per-mesh decode transform) and morph targets / blend shapes (section 40 is reserved and refused); a mesh holds at most 4 influences per vertex and 256 joints; the packing keeps one tangent frame (no per-vertex orthogonality check).
- **Phase 22 Day 320 (2026-11-14, Linux): open until the Linux machine and the Mac rebuild `clip.cpp`.** The Windows PC (2026-11-14) found that MSVC refuses `engine/asset/clip.cpp` (C4334, an error: a 32-bit `1u << c` widened to 64 bits against the `u64` channel mask, at the five mask tests of `ClipView::Open`, `IsConstant` and `Sample`); the shifts are `1ull << ...` now, the same values, and the clip tests pass in all four Windows trees. The Linux machine and the Mac rebuild and run `clip_test.cpp` on the change. The minted clip bytes (`f64` arithmetic, `Sin`/`Acos`/`Sqrt`) have not been compared across machines.
- **Phase 22 Day 320 (2026-11-14, Linux): planned and not done.** (1) The evaluator (blend, additive, root motion, event dispatch) is Day 321; `ClipView::Sample` is a reference sampler per track (binary search per call, no cursor). (2) `asset_mint mint-clip` takes the default `ClipLimits`; Day 339 wires the tool to the `assets.anim_*` settings. (3) `asset_mint remint` for clips and the golden-fixture upgrade test wait for Day 333. (4) `ClipView::Open` does not recompute the stored measured error (the minter guarantees it) and does not check constant or keyed rotations beyond finiteness and unit length of constants. (6) `ClpReduce` (the greedy split) is O(n log n) on noisy keys but O(n^2) on a smooth monotone channel of 65535 keys (each split peels one key); not measured. (7) The load and store helpers of `clip.cpp`, `mesh.cpp` and `skeleton.cpp` are three copies of one little-endian accessor pair; to share in a later day. (5) Key times are quantized to 1/65535 of the duration: a clip of many minutes has a time step of several milliseconds (no per-clip time scale choice).
- **Phase 22 Day 320 (2026-11-14, Linux): out of scope for v1, filed for Phase 31.** Retargeting a clip to another skeleton, curves other than linear (cubic and step interpolation of imported clips), morph-target and property tracks, compression of a clip across skeletons; at most 256 tracks, 65535 keys per channel and 1024 events.
- **Phase 22 Day 321 (2026-11-14, Linux): planned and not done.** (1) The benchmark figures are from a Debug build; the Release figure and the user's confirmation of the 4 ms target are Day 340's. (2) No blend tree, layering or state machine: the system plays one clip per entity and refuses additive clips; layering several players is for the game or a later phase. (3) `RootMotionOver` composes at most 64 whole loop passes of rotation. (4) Components hold raw `u64` ids (Day 327 declared the stored fields references; the members stay `u64`); the palette is a per-entity `Vector`, not a pooled chunk-friendly store. (5) Negative speed (reverse playback) counts as 0. (6) The system's poses live on the worker stack (about 32 KB per entity update).
- **Phase 22 Day 322 (2026-11-14, Linux): GCC unity build of `engine/net` fails (seen, not caused by the day).** `-Wsubobject-linkage` (an error) in `engine/net/async.cpp:37` (the `RetryWhileBlocked` coroutine frames hold a lambda from an anonymous namespace) and in `engine/net/sim_transport.cpp:168` (`SimulatedTransport::Impl` holds `Vector<UniquePtr<{anonymous}::Conn>>`), for the `net`, `net_asan` and `net_tsan` targets; `cmake --build -- -k 0` on a GCC unity tree builds everything else (renderer, scene, animation and their tests) clean. GCC non-unity and Clang in both modes are clean. Not investigated; the day touches no `engine/net` file. **Seen again 2026-11-20 (Linux, GCC unity, target `net`, after the VSE rename):** the same `-Wsubobject-linkage` errors at `engine/net/async.cpp:37` (the compiler's line; the source line there is the closing brace of the coroutine); GCC non-unity (`build-gcc`) builds `net` clean.
- **Phase 22 Day 322 (2026-11-14, Linux): planned and not done.** (1) `renderer.joint_palette_bytes` (default 16 MiB, `DeviceReset`, restart required) is declared and described, and `FrameResources` takes a matrix count, but nothing converts the setting into that count yet: `Renderer` and the demos use the default of 1024 matrices; wiring it is the day that builds the GPU-backed loaders (Day 329). (2) A mesh whose material is not a skinned one (or the reverse) is rejected and counted, not drawn with an error material: the plan's error-material fallback is a Day 325-326 material feature. (3) The GPU test's CPU reference is hand-written in the test, not the `animation` module's `SkinningMatrices`; the two agree on the Day 321 layout by `static_assert`s on the stride only. (4) Non-uniform scale in a pose is out of scope: normals use the weighted palette rotation without an inverse-transpose. (5) Compute pre-skinning (one skinning pass shared by shadow and depth passes) is a later optimization; every pass that draws a skinned mesh re-skins it in the vertex shader. (6) A `RenderObject` borrows its palette pointer (from the ECS `SkeletonInstance`): it is valid only while no structural ECS change happens between `RenderSync` and `GBufferPass::Prepare` (the frame order the guide states); the renderer does not copy it earlier. (7) Instances that share one pose each get their own copy of the palette in the frame's joint buffer (1,000 instances of a 40-joint pose use 1,000 x 40 matrices); sharing one copy per distinct palette pointer is a later optimization. (8) Benchmark figures (`[.][render-bench][skinning]`, Vulkan, Debug build, whole frame with readback): 1,000 instances of 4,900 vertices and 40 joints 12.08 ms (1 draw), 10,000 instances 32.43 ms (1 draw); the Release figures are Day 340's.
- **Phase 22 Day 323 (2026-11-14, Linux): planned and not done or declined.** (1) The plan's `TextureImage`-compatible copying overload of the loader is not written: `TextureView::Level` plus `CreateFromLevels` is the only entry, and the test builds its `TextureLevelRef`s from the view. (2) A cube map is created as a 6-layer 2D array (`rhi::TextureDimension::Cube` is reserved in the RHI); the cube view and the filtering across face seams come with the materials (Day 326). Faces are filtered independently, so seams can show at low mips. (3) The Kaiser filter's taps are placed from the start of each destination texel's range, so a range of three source texels (an odd size) is sampled half a texel off-center; the box filter is exact for every size. (4) `asset_mint mint-texture` reads each raw image whole (up to 2 GiB per file) and holds every layer's mip chain in memory. (5) Block-compressed formats are Days 324-325; 3D (volume) textures, the BGRA and 32-bit float formats, and `adopt` of the `id` line (Day 333) are not in this version. (6) The texture target tags 0, 1 and 2 are defined here (development and Vulkan, Direct3D 12, Metal); `pack --target` on Day 333 must use the same numbers, and a pak made for another tag is refused by `TextureLayoutForTarget`.

The failures were the Metal library test (the placeholder, minted by the Mac at `3536d792`; it passes after the pull), six display tests while the console was locked, and filed flakes that passed 3 of 3 reruns (`KNOWN-ISSUES.md`); `ctest -L docs` 7 of 7, `docs/reference/` regenerated unchanged. A BC upload to a real device has still not run. **The Mac's half is done** (2026-11-14, `72bf85f0`): the four macOS trees (x86-64 and the arm64 compile-only check, unity and non-unity) built clean, including the `BC4_UNorm` Metal mapping; `scripts/test-phase.sh --affected` `ran 2878 of 7962 tests (phases: all; flavors: plain; ...)`, asan 2440, tsan 2264, no failure; `ctest -L docs` 7 of 7. No BC texture was sampled by the Mac's GPU. **Windows PC (2026-11-15):** built and run in the Phase 22 closing set (the closing entry above). **The GPU decode (2026-11-15, the Windows PC):** the new `tests/rhi/bc_gpu_test.cpp` (phase 22; `rhi_tests` now link `asset`) encodes a 16x16 image to BC1, BC4, BC5 and BC7, uploads it as a compressed texture, samples it texel for texel and compares with `asset::DecodeImage`. On the Radeon 860M, D3D12 and Vulkan alike: BC7 matches exactly; BC1, BC4 and BC5 differ by up to **two** steps (807, 918 and 818 of 1,024 channels exact), not the one step `block_compress.h` claims. The engine's decoder truncates its interpolated values; whether it should round instead (which would change the encoder's choices and so the minters' golden hashes) is the user's decision. The test allows 2 for those three and 0 for BC7. Not run: the Linux machine's Vulkan (NVIDIA) and the Mac's Metal. **Rounding tried and not kept** (a throwaway build, 2026-11-15): with every palette division rounded (`+ d / 2`) in `Bc1Palette` and `Bc4Palette`, the worst difference is still 2 on both backends, and the channels off by 2 go from 19 to 11 for BC1 but from 19 to 28 for BC4 and 39 to 46 for BC5 (of 1,024). The GPU follows neither formula, so rounding does not buy the one-step claim; the header now says up to two steps (the user's choice, 2026-11-15; `block_compress.h`, `docs/guide/assets.md`), no minted byte changed.
- **Phase 22 Day 324 (2026-11-14, Linux): recorded, not changed.** (1) The BC1 `Fast` fit can be worse than a flat block on pure noise; Normal and High are not (tested). (3) A compressed texture's `alpha straight` default turns texels below alpha 128 transparent in BC1 (`punchthrough_alpha`); use `alpha ignore` for an opaque color texture with stray alpha. (4) BC3 and BC2 have decoders but no encoder and no `rhi::Format` for BC2.
