- **Phase 22 Day 332 (2026-11-14, Linux): open until the other machines run it, and deferred.** (1) `engine/asset/network_backend.*`, `blob_cache.*`, the `Changes` request and `tests/asset/network_backend_test.cpp` and `network_socket_test.cpp` (`asset_tests_sockets`, label `network`, real loopback sockets with a server thread) are built and run on the Linux machine only; the Windows and Mac runs are open. (2) `BlobCache` rewrites its whole index on every `Put` and looks names up linearly: a cache of tens of thousands of ids has not been measured. (3) The held table is not stored on disk: an offline start has no table until the server has been reached once, although the blobs are cached. (4) A range reader (a resource above `assets.net.prefetch_bytes`) is not cached and fails with `Offline` when the server goes away. (5) The backend answers `Busy` like a failure and retries it with the back-off; a server that stays busy makes the backend `Offline` and the reconnect then closes and reopens the connection. (6) The asynchronous table read ends with an error that says to read again when the table changes between two pages; the pages already handed out are not taken back. (7) The sockets test of a stopped server does not close the listening socket (the server thread ends, the kernel still accepts): it proves the timeouts and `Offline`, not a refused connection. **The Mac, plain only** (2026-11-15, `453a6dac`; the asan and tsan runs skipped at the user's request, pending the Mac's slowdown): the four macOS trees (x86-64 and the arm64 compile-only check, unity and non-unity) built clean from scratch; `scripts/test-phase.sh --affected --flavor plain` `ran 3126 of 8610 tests (phases: all; flavors: plain; display: included; crash tests: excluded) in 499 s`, failing only the flaky `Throughput scales with participant count` (passed 3 of 3 reruns); `ctest -L docs` 7 of 7. **Windows PC (2026-11-15):** built and run in the Phase 22 closing set (the closing entry above).
- **Phase 22 Day 333 (2026-11-14, Linux): open until the other machines run it, and deviations.** (1) `tools/asset_mint/project.*`, `check_mint.cmake`, `tests/asset/mint_sample` and `tests/asset/mint_determinism_test.cpp` are built and run on the Linux machine only; the Windows PC and the Mac have not built them (the sample's raw image is a binary file; the script tests copy the sample with CMake). (2) A project rule has the one option `skip`; the plan's per-extension options (a texture's format and quality) are lines in the texture source itself, because the minters take their options from the source and have no defaults to override. (3) `verify` does not check a base pak by hash: the project file names no base pak yet. (4) A source whose referenced blob changed (a texture's bytes, a skeleton's) is minted again and its file written only if the bytes differ; the plan says such a dependent is not rebuilt. The minters read the referenced blobs' metadata (a material checks the texture's shape and color space), so the tool cannot tell which changes matter without each minter reporting what it read. (5) References are found by reading words (`@HEX16`, ids after `skeleton`, words starting with `/`, a material's `texture` path): a string in a level that happens to be a path adds a dependency that is not one, and a reference that names nothing outside the `skeleton`, `include`, `animation` and `texture` lines is reported by the minter, not at load. (6) `adopt` writes only the `id` line: the `skeleton` id of a mesh or clip is written by hand. (7) The unchanged check reads and hashes every blob file on every `mint` (the plan asks for the file to be intact); a project of many gigabytes pays that read. **The Mac, plain only** (2026-11-15, `453a6dac`; the asan and tsan runs skipped at the user's request, pending the Mac's slowdown): the four macOS trees (x86-64 and the arm64 compile-only check, unity and non-unity) built clean from scratch; `scripts/test-phase.sh --affected --flavor plain` `ran 3126 of 8610 tests (phases: all; flavors: plain; display: included; crash tests: excluded) in 499 s`, failing only the flaky `Throughput scales with participant count` (passed 3 of 3 reruns); `ctest -L docs` 7 of 7. **Windows PC (2026-11-15):** built and run in the Phase 22 closing set (the closing entry above).
- **Out of plan, command line (2026-11-14, Linux): open until the other machines run it, and two declined review findings.** (1) `tools/metal_mint/macos/metal_mint.mm` was converted to `os::CommandLine` without being built (a Mac is needed); the Windows and Mac builds of `engine/foundation/os/command_line.h` and of the converted programs are open. (2) Declined: every demo builds a throwaway `os::CommandLine` to read the program name for its usage text before `DemoSettings::Begin` (one copy of `argv` at launch; the alternative is to give `Begin` the usage format, which changes its interface for no gain). (3) Declined: `docgen`'s `main` parses the command word with an undeclared `CommandLine` and each command parses again with its own declared options (an option is validated by the command that owns it, after dispatch; `tokens` takes no option and says so).
- **Phase 22 Day 334 (2026-11-14, Linux): open until the other machines run it, and deviations.** (1) `tools/asset_mint/target_transform.*`, `upgrade.*`, the new commands, `asset::SetBlobTarget` and `tests/asset/mint_edit_test.cpp` are built and run on the Linux machine only; the Windows PC and the Mac have not built them. (2) No built-in type has an older version (all are at version 1), so there is no real old-version blob and no registered upgrade step; `asset_mint_old_versions_fixture` tests the mechanism with a synthetic type. When a type's version is raised its author adds the step in `upgrade.cpp` and a golden old blob to the tests; until then `remint DIRECTORY` of a current folder reports "0 upgraded". (3) The level text minter is super-linear in the number of entities: 2,000 entities mint in 0.23 s, 4,000 in 0.85 s (debug), 20,000 in 20 s (debug) and 0.44 s in release; a level of 100,000 entities was measured by scaling the load figures, not by minting it. (4) `rename` and `move` rewrite the references that name the asset by path in the other sources (blobs hold ids, so no blob changes, but a dependent's source file does); a reference built some other way than a word that is a path (a string field that happens to be one) is rewritten too. (5) The table entries of a project output keep one path per id in a pak (`PathsOf` has several for an id with aliases). (6) The texture transform lays the rows out again for the target's alignment; it does not re-mint from the source (the context offers the source for a later BC7 to ASTC transform). (7) The Day 333 pack bug (an empty pak for a project) was found by this day's tests; any pak made by Day 333 is empty and must be packed again. **The Mac, plain only** (2026-11-15, `453a6dac`; the asan and tsan runs skipped at the user's request, pending the Mac's slowdown): the four macOS trees (x86-64 and the arm64 compile-only check, unity and non-unity) built clean from scratch; `scripts/test-phase.sh --affected --flavor plain` `ran 3126 of 8610 tests (phases: all; flavors: plain; display: included; crash tests: excluded) in 499 s`, failing only the flaky `Throughput scales with participant count` (passed 3 of 3 reruns); `ctest -L docs` 7 of 7. **Windows PC (2026-11-15):** built and run in the Phase 22 closing set (the closing entry above).
- **Phase 22 Day 335 (2026-11-15, Linux): open until the other machines run it, and deviations.** (1) `engine/asset_import`, `third_party/yyjson` (a C library, built with the project's flags per flavor; its own warnings are off, as for SQLite), `tools/json_boundary_check` and the glTF tests are built and run on the Linux machine only; the Windows PC and the Mac have not built them (the macOS branch of the root `CMakeLists.txt` names the module but has not been configured on a Mac). (2) `ImportGltf` imports meshes from Day 336 (the other kinds, Days 337-338). (3) The JSON nesting limit (128) is `YYJSON_READER_DEPTH_LIMIT` in `third_party/yyjson/CMakeLists.txt`, a compile-time definition of the vendored library, not a field of `ImportLimits`; the settings `assets.import.*` are Day 339. (4) A `.glb` BIN chunk is copied into its buffer (a second copy of the file's bytes while the document lives). (5) A large allocation that fails is reported (`LimitExceeded`) only on the paths that use `TryResize`; a small `Push` that runs out of memory still aborts, as everywhere in the engine. (6) The gcc unity build of `engine/net` still fails (above); `asset_import` builds in unity mode with GCC. **The Mac, plain only** (2026-11-15, `453a6dac`; the asan and tsan runs skipped at the user's request, pending the Mac's slowdown): the four macOS trees (x86-64 and the arm64 compile-only check, unity and non-unity) built clean from scratch; `scripts/test-phase.sh --affected --flavor plain` `ran 3126 of 8610 tests (phases: all; flavors: plain; display: included; crash tests: excluded) in 499 s`, failing only the flaky `Throughput scales with participant count` (passed 3 of 3 reruns); `ctest -L docs` 7 of 7. **Windows PC (2026-11-15):** built and run in the Phase 22 closing set (the closing entry above).
- **Phase 22 Day 336 (2026-11-15, Linux): meshes, deviations and limits.** (1) Tangents of a mesh without `TANGENT` come from the texture-coordinate derivatives summed per vertex, orthonormalized, with the bitangent sign from the mapping's handedness: deterministic, **not MikkTSpace**, so a normal map baked against MikkTSpace shades slightly differently on a mesh that has no tangents of its own (a mesh with `TANGENT` keeps the file's). (2) `--bake-nodes` (node transforms baked into the meshes) is not built: a mesh is imported in its own space, and the node hierarchy arrives as the `level` on Day 338. (3) The skeleton binding of a skinned mesh (`MeshSkeletonBinding`) is supplied by Day 337; until then `ImportGltf` imports a skinned primitive unskinned with a warning, and the skin path is tested through `ImportMeshes` directly. (4) `ImportGltf` counts skins, animations, materials, textures and images in a file as a warning (`N not imported yet`) until Days 337-338. (5) Primitives of one mesh may not exceed 64 (the mesh format's slots); a mesh with more is refused `TooLarge`. (6) Two primitives that use one material get distinct slots (`primitive<N>` for the second), so a material is bound once per slot, not once per name. (7) Open until the Windows PC and the Mac build `gltf_mesh.*` and its tests.
- **Phase 22 Day 337 (2026-11-15, Linux): skeletons and clips, deviations and limits.** (1) A mesh that nodes instance with two different skins is refused (`two skins`), though glTF allows it; importing it once per skin is not built. (2) A clip channel of a joint whose bind pose folds in a non-joint node with a shear or an uneven scale is dropped with a warning (the keys cannot follow it); exact for rotation-translation-uniform-scale ancestors. (3) Clip key times are `u16` ticks of the clip's duration, so a clip of very long duration cannot hold a sharp `STEP` (a step's one-tick jump is merged into the later key when both round to one float time, and the clip minter's measured-error check refuses what it cannot hold). (4) `CUBICSPLINE` is resampled to linear keys at `clip_rate` (30 Hz); the largest error is reported (`resampling_translation_error`, `resampling_rotation_degrees`) and not bounded. (5) The joint's `skeleton` property of a skin (the root joint hint) is ignored; skeleton joints are the skin's joints and their parents among them. (6) `max_skins` joints per file are bounded by memory only through `max_output_bytes`. (7) Open until the Windows PC and the Mac build the Day 337 files (`gltf_nodes`, `gltf_skin`, `gltf_clip`, `gltf_names`).
- **Phase 22 Day 338 (2026-11-15, Linux): images, textures, materials, the async entry and the command; deviations and open items.** (1) **stb_image's JPEG decoder fails on some valid progressive files** (found by the tests: a quality-95 4:2:0 progressive file from libjpeg's `cjpeg` fails with `bad huffman code`, quality 75 decodes); libjpeg-turbo is the recorded fallback (`third_party/stb_image/VENDORED.md`); baseline JPEGs, 12-bit and arithmetic-coded ones are unaffected or unsupported as documented. CMYK/Adobe JPEGs are not tested. (2) The standard material template has scalar metallic and roughness, no occlusion or emissive map and no texture transform: those glTF properties are dropped with a counted warning (a transform is refused); the normal map is BC7 (the shader reads xyz), not BC5. (3) **Not built, moved out of Day 338:** `--emit-level` (a glTF scene as a level: the level minter is tool code over the ECS and was not moved), registering the glTF in an `assets.project` and re-minting from it (`asset_mint import-gltf` writes blobs and a manifest; the project integration, the incremental cache key and the "removed object fails `mint`" rule are not wired), and the golden-image render of an imported model. (4) A mesh instanced with two skins and a texture used both as color and as normal are handled by the Day 337 rule and by two texture resources. (5) Only the Linux machine built and ran this; the vendored C libraries (zlib `Z_SOLO`, libpng without SIMD, stb) are untried on the Windows PC and the Mac. (6) **ThreadSanitizer cannot follow libpng's `longjmp`** (`ThreadSanitizer: can't find longjmp buf` in the first test that makes libpng report an error): the two image tests that feed libpng hostile PNGs (`the limits are checked...`, `truncated and mutated files...`) skip under tsan; plain and asan run them (the other image and import tests run under tsan). (7) The decoder boundary has no gate of its own yet (`png.h`, `stb_image.h` are listed in `module_deps_check`'s include dirs); the Day 339 `asset_boundary_check` should cover them.
- **Phase 22 Day 339 (2026-11-15, Linux): built, and moved out.** Built: `assets.import.*` settings (`ImportLimitsFromSettings`), the gate `asset_boundary_check` (with fixtures), `game/asset_demo` (the in-memory import path, headless). **Not built, moved out of Day 339:** the animated demo (blending, root motion, a palette HUD), the three-backend render comparison with screenshots (loose, pak, server) and its golden image, `--list-backends`, `--simulated`, `--break` (the dependency demo), `--reset-after` (device reset in the demo), the hot reload of loose files in the manager, `docs/tutorials/loading-a-level.md`, and the gate's rule against loading a blob by a raw `ResourceId` literal. The rest of the `assets.*` settings of the day's list (mounts, cache, in-flight, network) already existed from Days 315-332. Linux only.
- **Phase 22 Day 340 (2026-11-15, Linux): the sweep of what the phase left out.** Limits filed by the phase, in one place: paks and blobs are not compressed; no ASTC (BC1, BC4, BC5 and BC7 mode 6 for encoding; the decoders read the other BC7 modes); no network write path and no encryption (the user, 2026-11-12); glTF: only the extensions the importer lists (`KHR_materials_emissive_strength`, `KHR_texture_transform` as the identity), no `KHR_mesh_quantization`, no Draco or meshopt, no sparse-morph targets (morph targets are refused or dropped), tangents are not MikkTSpace, a scene is not imported as a level, one skin per mesh, one sampler per material; clips are linear keys (cubic splines are resampled) with no retargeting, events or root-motion extraction; the standard material template has no occlusion, emissive or metallic-roughness map. **Pass conditions** (`docs/superpowers/specs/2026-11-13-asset-pipeline-design.md`, "Results"): the id and path lookups, BC1 and BC7 encoding speed and the animation update are met on the Linux machine; the pak against loose read rate, the 90 % load-path rate, the 10,000-resource level residency and frame impact, the bulk component load, the asynchronous latency and 40-joint characters were not measured, and the conditions are not yet confirmed by the user. Benchmarks ran in Release on Linux only.
- **Phase 22 Day 341 (2026-11-15, Linux): findings of the uncapped phase review that were recorded, not fixed** (each a verified-by-reading report; none has a failing test yet). (1) `MountTable::StartLocked` calls `ReadTableAsync` under the mount table's mutex, and `NetworkBackend::ReadTableAsync` can complete an old ticket on that thread, whose callback (`OnTicketDone`) takes the same non-recursive mutex: a possible deadlock with a network mount whose table read is restarted; the tests call `ReadTableAsync` without a mount callback and do not reach it. (2) `LoadGpuMesh` and the CPU `LoadMesh` do not run `MeshView::CheckIndices` and `CheckSkin` (a tool's job, Day 319): a hostile blob with valid header and sizes can hand the GPU out-of-range indices or joint numbers; the content hash is checked only with `assets.verify_on_load`. (3) A by-path follower request takes the Cancelled result of a leader whose own tickets were all dropped. (4) `BlobCache::Open` hashes every cached blob on the calling thread, `Put` rewrites the whole index, lookups are linear, and `Read` does not re-hash. (5) `LoadFallback` compares a loader key of 0 differently from `AcquireLocked`; the blob size limit is `2^32` inclusive in the writers and exclusive in the readers. (6) `ResourceServer` rescans a directory backend on every table or list request. (7) `SkeletonView::Open` trusts the header's skeleton hash, which `ClipView::Bind` and `MeshView::CheckSkeleton` treat as proof. (8) `ClipView::Sample` returns a keyed rotation at a boundary key unnormalized when its stored components exceed unit length. (9) `LoadBufferPool` rounds a request up to a power of two and charges the rounded size. (10) A source whose relative path holds `#` is dropped from the project manifest on reload (the manifest is tokenized with `#` as a comment). Fixed in the same review: the manifest-independent items listed in the Day 341 file (the server's use of a connection after a failed reply, the client's trust of the server's read size, an undefined cast, the back-off shift, a handle used before its validity check, and a hand-indexed `argv`).
- **Phase 22 closing (2026-11-15, Linux): what the Windows PC and the Mac must still run.** The Phase 22 closing set on `windows-msvc` (plain and asan), `windows-clang-cl` and `macos-clang` (plain, asan, tsan, plus the `macos-clang-arm64` compile): the full suite; the texture, mesh, clip, material and importer determinism tests (their golden hashes must match the Linux machine's); the vendored C libraries (`third_party/zlib` with `Z_SOLO`, `libpng`, `stb_image`, `yyjson`) and `asset_import`; the `asset_mint` script tests (`asset_mint_gltf` included); the skinned shaders' DXIL and `.metallib` outputs. Until then every Phase 22 day from 313 is open on those machines (the earlier per-day entries stay as they are). **The Windows half is done** (the Windows PC, `master` at `28c5ef04` plus the C4611 fix (2026-11-15): the four trees (MSVC and clang-cl, unity and non-unity) built clean; `scripts/test-phase.sh --all --flavor all`: `build-msvc` `ran 5974 of 5974 tests (phases: all; flavors: all (plain and asan, no tsan on Windows); display: included; crash tests: included) in 479 s`, `build-windows-msvc-nounity` `ran 5974 of 5974 tests (phases: all; flavors: all (plain and asan, no tsan on Windows); display: included; crash tests: included) in 483 s`; `--all --flavor plain`: `build-clang-cl` `ran 3257 of 3257 tests (phases: all; flavors: plain; display: included; crash tests: included) in 486 s`, `build-windows-clang-cl-nounity` `ran 3257 of 3257 tests (phases: all; flavors: plain; display: included; crash tests: included) in 469 s` (run before the C4611 fix; after it the clang-cl trees reran the 375 asset and importer tests and the 60 gate tests, all passing). The only failures were the six display tests (`Windows clipboard: ...` four, `Windows drag and drop: a dropped CF_HDROP ...`, `Windows: the left Alt is Alt, ...`), failing in every tree and flavor with the console locked (`LogonUI.exe` running, no Remote Desktop session), and `FramePool does not commit slab memory until blocks are used [asan]` once (filed flaky; 3 of 3 reruns passed). The minters' golden hashes match the Linux machine's (the determinism tests pass), the vendored C libraries and `asset_import` build and pass, the `asset_mint` script tests pass, and `cmake --build build-msvc --target shaders` re-mints every DXIL byte-identical. `ctest -L docs` 7 of 7, `docs/reference/` unchanged. The demos ran inside the suite; the windowed demos were not run by hand (the console was locked); the six display tests that failed with the console locked were rerun on 2026-11-15 with the desktop unlocked: all six pass in every tree and flavor (14 of 14 in each MSVC tree, plain and asan; 7 of 7 in each clang-cl tree), and `rhi_demo`, `renderer_demo` and `ui_demo` ran 1000 frames with validation on DX12 and Vulkan, no message).
- **Phase 22 closing (2026-11-15, the Windows PC): two MSVC build errors fixed, open until the Linux machine and the Mac build them.** (1) MSVC refused `engine/asset_import/image_decode.cpp` with C4611 ("interaction between '_setjmp' and C++ object destruction is non-portable", an error): libpng reports errors by `longjmp`, so the reader called `setjmp` in C++. The one `setjmp` is now in a C file, `engine/asset_import/png_guard.c` (`engine_asset_png_guard(png, body, context)`), and `image_decode.cpp` runs the header read and the decode as bodies under it (plain data only, as before); `asset_boundary_check` lets `png_guard.c` include `png.h` (the user chose this fix, 2026-11-15). The Linux branch of `engine/asset_import/CMakeLists.txt` now applies the production options (`-fno-rtti`, ...) to C++ only, as the Windows branch did, so the C file builds there. (2) `tests/renderer/reset_soak_test.cpp:246` narrowed the `usize` texture count to `u32` (C4267); it is a `usize`. Both build clean in the four Windows trees and the asset, importer and gate tests pass; the Linux machine (Clang and GCC, `-Wall -Wextra -Werror` on a C file) and the Mac have not built them.
- **Phase 23 Day 342 (2026-11-15, Linux): open until the other machines run it.** The option `ENGINE_BUILD_SHARED` and its test (`engine_shared_option`) are shared CMake code; the Windows PC and the Mac have not configured it. The spikes ran on Linux x86-64 with Clang only (GCC, Windows DLL and macOS dylib behavior of the singleton, visibility and unload findings are to be confirmed on Days 354 and 355). **Windows PC (2026-11-15):** the four trees configured with the option off; `engine_shared_option` passes in the full runs.
- **Phase 23 Day 343 (2026-11-15, Linux): deviations and open items.** (1) The second set of the engine's own test suites linked against the shared library moved to Day 356 (the mode argument of `engine_add_*_tests` is added where the suites are run shared). (2) `libengine.so` has hard `NEEDED` entries on `libwayland-client`, `libX11`, `libxkbcommon`, `libXcursor`, `libXi` and `libXfixes` (the platform and window modules are whole-archived): a host without them cannot load it; Day 352 decides (lazy `dlopen` of the window libraries, or the window host outside the aggregate). (3) With the option ON the static suites, tools and demos are also compiled position independent and hidden (one global setting), so an ON configuration differs from the default in more than the library. (4) Release sizes and start-up are Day 356's. (5) GCC unity cannot build the shared library because `engine/net` fails under GCC unity (known). (6) Open until the Windows PC and the Mac run the option check; the shared target itself is Linux only until Days 354 and 355.
- **Phase 23 Day 344 (2026-11-15, Linux): open until the other machines run it.** `abi_check` reads the library's symbols with GNU `nm -D` (Linux); the Windows (`dumpbin /exports`) and macOS (`nm -gU`) readers arrive with Days 354 and 355, and the `ENGINE_API` form for the MSVC family (`dllexport`) is untried until then. The list-against-git rule is skipped, with a notice, outside a git checkout.
- **Phase 23 Day 345 (2026-11-15, Linux): open until the other machines run it, and two recorded limits.** (1) `shared_state_check`, the `thread_local` conversions (`ReleaseThreadAlternateStack` has empty Windows and macOS definitions, `NotifyThreadEnd`) and the changed headers are built and run on the Linux machine only; the Windows PC and the Mac run them no later than Day 357. (2) Every thread that used the engine must call `NotifyThreadEnd` before it ends: the frame-pool cache, the crash alternate stack and the Vulkan command-pool slot are plain data given back there (job workers, the I/O thread and a scheduler's creating thread do; the C API's `engine_thread_detach` is for host threads, Day 346). A thread that does not strands up to 64 cached blocks per size class, keeps its Vulkan slot (`kThreadSlots`: the next thread to record may find none) and a 128 KiB alternate stack. (3) The ownership test counts blocks in the engine; under ASan the host's `free` of an engine block was not reported (the library binds its own `operator new`).

- **Phase 23 Day 345 follow-up (2026-11-15, Linux): flaky under load, not caused by the change.** `The frame loop on an X11 window keeps frames in flight bounded and leaks nothing` (phase 4) failed once in `scripts/test-phase.sh --affected` (plain, `ran 3341 of 8864 tests`) and passed when run alone and in the next full affected run.

- **Phase 23 Day 346 (2026-11-15, Linux): open until the other machines run it, and three recorded limits.** (1) The C header, the `abi_check` directive and the status map are built and run on the Linux machine only; the Windows PC and the Mac check them no later than Day 357. (2) `ENGINE_C_TEST_EXTENSION` defaults to ON, so a library built today carries `engine.test_state` (crash in a job, raw allocation); Day 357 decides the release default. (3) The destroy/create race fix has no test (needs a second thread inside the observer wait). The `status unreleased` line in `abi/engine.exports` is removed at the first release (Day 357).

- **Phase 23 Day 347 (2026-11-15, Linux): open until the other machines run it; one failing test seen, since fixed.** (1) `engine_loader.h` is POSIX only; the Windows variant is Day 354, the Mac checks the POSIX one no later than Day 357; `abi_snapshot_gen`, the snapshot rules and `host_dl` run on Linux only for now. The snapshot is the 64-bit layout. (2) The BC GPU test failed on the Linux NVIDIA Vulkan device (worst channel difference 5 to 6, 810 of 1024 channels exact, against the 2 steps measured on the Radeon). **Fixed 2026-11-15 (Day 349):** `tests/rhi/bc_gpu_test.cpp` compares the mean squared difference (PSNR 40 dB for BC1, BC4 and BC5, 50 dB for BC7) instead of each channel; measured here about 0.95 (48 dB). Vendors round the block decode differently, so no per-channel bound is portable. **The Windows PC (2026-11-15):** the four Windows trees built clean at `7aa09bef`; `scripts/test-phase.sh --affected` `ran 3098 of 5997 tests (phases: all; flavors: plain; display: included; crash tests: excluded)` in both MSVC trees, `ran 3128 of 3279 tests (phases: all; flavors: plain; display: included; crash tests: excluded)` in both clang-cl trees, asan `ran 2618 of 5997 tests (phases: all; flavors: asan; display: included; crash tests: excluded)` in both MSVC trees; the failures were filed flakes and one new one (the resolver queue, below), each passing 3 of 3 reruns; `ctest -L docs` 7 of 7. Day 349 (`249d3f92`) is not built here yet.

- **Phase 23 Day 348 (2026-11-15, Linux): open until the other machines run it, and two recorded limits.** (1) The unload destructor (`__attribute__((destructor))`), the `/proc`-based reload test and `FramePool::DestroyGlobalAtUnload` are Linux only; the Windows DLL (`DllMain` detach) and macOS (`__attribute__((destructor))`) equivalents are Days 354 and 355. (2) The library's destructor runs at process exit as well as at `dlclose`: a host that releases engine frame memory after it has run breaks the contract (`thread_detach` and `destroy` first). (3) The outstanding-object refusal is tested through the test extension until Days 349-351 add real object kinds; each must register with `engine_objects.h`. **The Windows PC (2026-11-15):** built and run with Day 347 (that entry); the unload and reload paths themselves are Linux only until Day 354.
- **Phase 23 Day 349 (2026-11-15, Linux): open until the other machines run it; recorded limits.** (1) `tests/engine_library/host_runtime.c`, the runtime (`runtime.cpp`) and the crash-handler functions use POSIX threads, `fork` and `dirent` and run on Linux only; the Windows DLL and macOS dylib days (354, 355) check the C API there. (2) The four tsan SEGFAULTs filed on Days 320 and 322 are fixed: the NVIDIA driver starts threads TSan does not know (`CHECK failed: thr->slot != 0`, a crash in the driver's `calloc`), the repo's rule leaves `[gpu]` tests out of TSan on Linux, and four tests lacked it (two Vulkan vertex-format tests were not tagged, the scene tests had no `~[gpu]` filter); those four GPU tests therefore never run under TSan on this machine. (3) `jobs_submit` refuses past 2048 jobs queued or running (`LIMIT_EXCEEDED`), because the scheduler aborts when its queues are full; the number is below the scheduler's capacity, not measured. (4) The world calls and the frame share one lock, so a long frame holds up world calls from other threads (and from jobs); finer locking is not planned. (5) The component set is four kinds (transform, world transform, name, mesh reference with a host-chosen identifier); parents, other built-in components and resource handles come with Day 350. (6) `setting_get` of a text setting returns the value quoted (`FormatValue`), so `crash.dump_directory` reads back with quotes; not changed. **The Windows PC (2026-11-15):** the four Windows trees built clean at `c85e1e6b`; `scripts/test-phase.sh --affected` `ran 3099 of 6000 tests (phases: all; flavors: plain; display: included; crash tests: excluded)` in both MSVC trees, `ran 3129 of 3282 tests (phases: all; flavors: plain; display: included; crash tests: excluded)` in both clang-cl trees, asan `ran 2619 of 6000 tests (phases: all; flavors: asan; display: included; crash tests: excluded)` in both MSVC trees; only flakes failed (filed), each passing 3 of 3 reruns; `ctest -L docs` 7 of 7. The POSIX-only hosts and the X11/Wayland surfaces are not built on Windows (Day 354).
- **Phase 23 Day 350 (2026-11-15, Linux): open until the other machines run it; recorded limits.** (1) `tests/engine_library/host_resources.c` and the resource functions of the C API are built and run on Linux only until Days 354 and 355. (2) The C API mounts only the memory backend: paks and loose files named by `assets.mounts` are not mounted, and the resource settings (`assets.memory_backend_bytes`, `assets.memory_priority`, the manager's) are read once when the engine is created. (3) Loaded resources are the CPU kinds (skeleton, animation, mesh, texture, material, level); GPU resources come with the renderer's C functions. (4) A replace of a loaded resource rebuilds it in place; if the new blob cannot be loaded the old version stays and the call still returns OK (the review's finding 2, declined). (5) `release` callbacks run one after another on one job of the engine's job system: a callback that blocks holds up the hand-back of the others and `engine_destroy`. (6) The overwritten-lent-buffer check is the blob's own validation (`CORRUPT` at the load); a buffer that was freed and still holds the old bytes is not detected except by AddressSanitizer. (7) The 24-flip replace test measures nothing about speed; no time or memory was measured for adding thousands of resources (each add or replace marks the mounts' tables to be read again, once, before the next path load). **The Windows PC (2026-11-15):** the four Windows trees built clean at `c85e1e6b`; `scripts/test-phase.sh --affected` `ran 3099 of 6000 tests (phases: all; flavors: plain; display: included; crash tests: excluded)` in both MSVC trees, `ran 3129 of 3282 tests (phases: all; flavors: plain; display: included; crash tests: excluded)` in both clang-cl trees, asan `ran 2619 of 6000 tests (phases: all; flavors: asan; display: included; crash tests: excluded)` in both MSVC trees; only flakes failed (filed), each passing 3 of 3 reruns; `ctest -L docs` 7 of 7. The POSIX-only hosts and the X11/Wayland surfaces are not built on Windows (Day 354).
- **Host cache of imported resources (maintenance, 2026-11-18, Linux; `plans/maintenance/host-resource-cache-api.md`): open until the other machines run it; recorded limits.** (1) Built and run on Linux only (plain, asan, tsan): `resource_read`, `resource_add_many`, `resource_add_many_async` (+ `_wait`, `_finish`, `_destroy`), `import_format_version`, `ENGINE_CAP_RESOURCE_CACHE`, `engine_import_result.summary`, `HostMemoryBackend::AddMany`; the Windows PC and the Mac have not built or run them, and neither has a `-DENGINE_BUILD_SHARED=ON` or `-DENGINE_LIBRARY_WITH_IMPORTER=OFF` tree (the importer-less run is `engine_c_import_off`, built in the plain tree only through `engine_c_noimport`). (2) The speed of the synchronous add was not measured, so there is no figure for when the asynchronous form pays; the plan asked to measure first. (3) `summary` bounds and node positions are of the file's rest pose: skinning, morph targets and animation do not move them; a mesh whose POSITION accessor has no `min`/`max` is read element by element; a skinned avatar's height is the height of its bind pose. (4) `import_format_version` is a hash of the importer's output version, the container version and each type's version; the number in `host_import.c` (`EXPECTED_FORMAT_VERSION`) is updated by hand in the commit that moves any of them, and nothing yet checks that a change of what the importer writes also raised `kGltfImporterOutputVersion` (only a golden of the output would). (5) `resource_add_many` takes at most 65536 items, an asynchronous adding is limited to 256 tickets, and a ticket that is never finished or destroyed keeps the engine from being destroyed (`IN_USE`).
- **Phase 23 Day 351 (2026-11-15, Linux): open until the other machines run it; recorded limits.** (1) `tests/engine_library/host_import.c`, `host_import_off.c` and the import functions are built and run on Linux only until Days 354 and 355; `ENGINE_LIBRARY_WITH_IMPORTER` and the importer's libraries (zlib, libpng, the JPEG decoder) in the shared library are unchecked on Windows and macOS. (2) Re-importing over resources still in the backend is `ALREADY_EXISTS`: the host removes the old ones first (ids are kept through the manifest); there is no in-place re-import. (3) `import_finish` takes the outcome once: when adding the result fails (`ALREADY_EXISTS`), the import is lost and a second call is `NOT_FOUND`. (4) At most 256 import tickets exist at once. (5) The importer's limits are the `assets.import.*` settings read at create (or the per-import overrides). (6) `host_import` under ThreadSanitizer leaves the fixture's PNG uncorrupted (libpng's `longjmp`, see the known issue above); no measurement of import time or memory through the C API was taken. (7) The option defaults to ON in every configuration, not OFF outside the shared library as the day's task text said: the static `engine_c` of the tests needs it. **The Windows PC (2026-11-15):** the four Windows trees built clean at `c85e1e6b`; `scripts/test-phase.sh --affected` `ran 3099 of 6000 tests (phases: all; flavors: plain; display: included; crash tests: excluded)` in both MSVC trees, `ran 3129 of 3282 tests (phases: all; flavors: plain; display: included; crash tests: excluded)` in both clang-cl trees, asan `ran 2619 of 6000 tests (phases: all; flavors: asan; display: included; crash tests: excluded)` in both MSVC trees; only flakes failed (filed), each passing 3 of 3 reruns; `ctest -L docs` 7 of 7. - **Phase 23 Day 354 (2026-11-15, the Windows PC): `engine_c_runtime` hung twice in full parallel runs of `build-msvc`** (plain at about 18:00, asan from 18:35; each stopped by hand after 16 and 24 minutes; never in `build-windows-msvc-nounity` or the clang-cl trees; 24 of 24 runs alone, 8 at a time, passed). Stacks of the asan hang (a throwaway DbgHelp walker, kept in the session only): the main thread in `destroy` waits in `JobHost::~JobHost` (`runtime.cpp:152`) to join the job host thread, which is in `Scheduler::~Scheduler` -> `IoThread::Shutdown` (`async_io.cpp:71`) joining the I/O thread; one thread is exiting and waits in `LdrShutdownThread` (`FreeLibraryAndExitThread`, `endthreadex`), another is starting and waits in `LdrInitializeThunk`; the workers wait on their condition variable. Which thread holds the loader lock was not found. `engine_c_runtime` now has a 300-second timeout, so a hang fails the test. Again on 2026-11-16 at `6641b0af`, now in `build-windows-msvc-nounity` (plain `--affected`): stopped by its 300-second timeout; 3 of 3 reruns alone passed.
- **Phase 23 Day 354 (2026-11-15, the Windows PC): a fault inside a job ends the process as an abort on MSVC.** In `host_runtime`'s crash child the access violation inside a job of the C API reaches `Job::promise_type::unhandled_exception` (`engine/jobs/job.h:103`), whose assert aborts: exit code 3, a report with reason `Abort` whose stack starts in `unhandled_exception` (the faulting frame is not in it), and the dump written. On Linux the same child dies of SIGSEGV. The Windows side of `host_run_crash_child` takes exit code 3 or the access violation as the crash.

(3) The uncapped phase review's 17 recorded findings: outcome of a spent import or resource ticket when the backend add fails (`import_finish`, `TicketFinish`); a NULL `callbacks` in `surface_rebind` keeps the old ones; Cocoa: the view is not retained, a rebind to the same view drops the layer backing; handle generations wrap to 0 after 2^32 objects; `engine_buffer` release trusts the host-visible `size`; a host log callback that re-enters the API can deadlock on the runtime, surface and world mutexes; `exit()` with a live engine runs the unload hook under running workers; `engine_load` on Windows with a relative path uses `LOAD_WITH_ALTERED_SEARCH_PATH`; the test extension (`engine.test_state`) is compiled into the default shared library; `engine_create_info.app_name` is not read and `-h` is ignored; touch has no cancel and a stuck finger blocks the others; `ENGINE_KEY_UNKNOWN` events are refused; a string length above INT_MAX reaches a `%.*s`; a NULL path with a length is taken as no path in the resource calls; a Windows crash-gate filter can stay on the chain after the DLL unloads. - **Phase 27 Day 451 (2026-11-18, Linux): script world limits.** (1) The per-partition statistics have no instruction count: the backend interface reports none (the LuaJIT hook counts only while a budget is set); calls, time, budget hits, out-of-memory results, resets and memory are counted. Day 452 decides whether `CallOutput` carries an instruction count. (2) `math.random` is seeded by the backend (`script.random_seed`), the same for every partition; the spec's per-partition stream waits for the day the world seeds each state (Day 452). (3) A lease must not be held across a job suspension point; nothing checks it except the debug owner check of the state.

- **Phase 27 Day 458, deferred:** (1) no hot-script detector for a partition job that runs long without yielding; the per-callback instruction budget (`ScriptRuntimeConfig::budget`, default ten million) is the only bound. (2) The script system declares `ReadsAllComponents`/`WritesAllComponents` and so never overlaps another system; a behavior declaring its component access (so the scheduler could overlap script work with audio or culling) is not built. (3) A partition's instances run in one job, so one partition cannot use more than one thread; the evenness test shows no heavy partition for entity-id hashes.

- **Phase 27 Day 459, deferred:** (1) `event.call(entity, interface, fn)` is not built; the guide describes the request/reply event shape instead. (2) `on_tick`, `on_collision`, `on_trigger`, `on_input` and `on_asset_loaded` are not emitted yet: each is a native `EventBus::Emit` added by the day that owns the source (physics Day 466, input Day 468). (3) The log order of handler errors is covered by the per-partition log buffers, but no test captures the order (no log capture hook).

- **Phase 27 Day 460, deferred:** `wait_all`, `wait_any`, the timeout argument of `wait_event` and an `on_cancel` hook are not built (a state reset or hot reload cannot run script code in a dead state); `stop` ends a spawned task only when it next wakes, so a task waiting for an event that never comes lives until its entity is destroyed; a handler that waits delays that instance's other handlers for the same event. The Day 459 security review's resource limits (payload weight and subscription caps per partition) are in this commit: `kMaxEventPayloadWeight`, `kMaxSubscriptionChangesPerPartition`, `kMaxSubscriptionsPerPartition`.
- **Phase 27 Day 461, deferred:** a task runs at the end of the tick that asked for it, so a long task lengthens that tick (no multi-tick latency yet); a queued task cannot be cancelled by handle (only with its owner); results are kept until taken or the owner is destroyed (no timeout), so a script that never takes them fills `script.task_queue_limit`; `task.map` arrays and `parallel_for` chunk counts are capped at 65,536 by the value container limit, so `parallel_for` returns one result per chunk, not per index; the function of a task is source text only (minted script assets come with Days 462-463); tasks cannot be started from native code or from a task. Not run: macOS (Day 474); the task pool ran on the PC on Day 473.
- **Phase 27 Day 461, deferred (found in review):** (1) tasks have no total instruction budget per tick, only the per-chunk budget (`ScriptRuntimeConfig::task_budget`, 500 million), the queue limit and the argument-byte limit: a script that queues the most allowed chunks can use a tick's time on the task pool. (2) A task's source is still spliced into a wrapper chunk (mitigated by the announce/define/seal protocol and a second hash of the source); a `State` call that compiles a function without splicing would remove the path.
- **Phase 27 Day 462 (2026-11-18, Linux):** (1) `asset_mint mint-script` is the command's name (the plan said `asset_mint script`; every other minter is `mint-<kind>`), and it needs the LuaJIT backend, which builds on Linux and Windows x86-64 (Day 473) and on macOS (Day 474). (2) The run that lists a script's behaviors executes the top level of the source once with no engine bindings, so top-level code that calls one (`time.dt()`) fails the mint unless `--no-run` is given; the banned-name scan is by name only (`_G["io"]` and `rawget(_G, "os")` are the sandbox's to stop at run time). (3) The blob carries bytecode but nothing loads it yet (the `bytecode` capability of the backend stays false: Day 463). (4) No ctest script drives `asset_mint mint-script` through the command line; `tests/script/script_asset_test.cpp` tests the library the command calls. (5) Built and tested on Windows on Day 473 (MSVC and clang-cl, unity on and off, the shared library) and on the Mac on Day 474 (statically linked only).
- **Phase 27 Day 463 (2026-11-18, Linux):** done as the days' text asks except: (1) a script component still names a behavior (`ScriptComponent::behavior`), not a script asset id; `ScriptAssets::ScriptOf(name)` maps a behavior name to the script that defines it, and the component by id waits for a day that changes the component's saved form. (2) The development loader is only the setting `script.dev_directory` (declared, default empty, read by `ScriptDevDirectory`); `ScriptDevWatcher` (Day 464) reads it, and no gate yet keeps it out of a release build's defaults (the default is empty). (3) Asynchronous loading of scripts is the manager's (`Load<ScriptView>` through the async loader works the same as for any type) and has no test of its own; the pak and the resource server serve script blobs as bytes like every type, covered by their own tests with other types, with no script-specific test. (4) The bytecode in a blob is still not loaded (the backend's `bytecode` capability stays false); every state compiles from source. (5) A minted script that fails to define or run in a state leaves that state failed: every checkout of the partition reports the same error until the state is reset. (5a) fixed in the Day 464 commit after the security review: the module host's functions are kept by the backend outside the globals (`State::LoadHost`, `@name` calls) and each module gets its own `__require` in its own environment; the behavior host's `__behavior_*` functions are still globals of the state (one trust domain per state). (5b) Two scripts that define a behavior of one name are not detected: the later definition wins in the state and `ScriptAssets::ScriptOf` names the first. (6) Built and tested on Windows on Day 473 (MSVC and clang-cl, unity on and off, the shared library) and on the Mac on Day 474 (statically linked only).
- **Phase 27 Day 464 (2026-11-18, Linux):** hot reload and the directory watcher, with these limits: (1) the native watchers of the other platforms are written: Windows `ReadDirectoryChangesW` (Day 473) and macOS FSEvents (Day 474, its limits in the Day 474 entry). (2) The inotify backend reports a file when it is closed after writing, a move and a create; a process that keeps a file open and writes it without closing (a log) is not reported, and a file system without inotify (some network mounts) is not told apart from one with it: `IsNative()` is true and no events arrive. (3) The reload runs only between ticks on one thread, and does not run on a partition without a state (it takes the new text when made); it is not wired to a runtime setting or an engine frame loop: a program calls `ScriptReloader::Reload` itself (the day's tests do), and no demo does. (4) A behavior removed from a script keeps its old class until its instances are destroyed; a coroutine waiting across a reload finishes as the old text wrote it. (5) `script.hot_reload` is a restart-required setting with no gate that forces it off in a shipped build (its default is off). (6) `asset_mint mint-script --dev-out=DIR` was run by hand once; no ctest drives it. (8) A failed reload restores module values and behavior classes, not globals that the new text assigned before it failed (a plain `function f()` in a reloaded script stays redefined in the partitions that had run it). (7) The behavior host's `__behavior_reload_commit` now clears the caller identity after each hook (a review finding); the identity was already unobservable after the commit, so the regression test passes with and without that change and proves only that no identity is visible.
- **Phase 27 Day 474 (2026-11-19, the Mac): LuaJIT, the FSEvents watcher and the script tests on macOS; not run, not built and limits.** (1) Plain only: the asan and tsan flavors the day file asks for were not run (the Mac skips sanitizer runs, the user's rule of 2026-11-15), so the script tests' multithreaded suites have no ThreadSanitizer run on macOS. (2) Built later on the Mac (2026-11-19, container audit Day 12): the nojit tree (`ENGINE_LUAJIT_JIT=OFF`, the four script test programs only: 242, 35, 19 and 5 cases pass) and `-DENGINE_BUILD_SHARED=ON` (the plain host tests of `tests/engine_library/` only: 48 of 48, `engine_c_script` and `engine_c_script_sample` among them). Not built: a whole `-DENGINE_LIBRARY_WITH_IMPORTER=OFF` configuration (`engine_c_import_off` passed in the shared tree). (3) arm64 is compile-only: `build-macos-arm64` built clean (again on 2026-11-19, unity; the Mac builds no unity-off tree from that day, the user's rule); in `build-macos-arm64-nounity` every source compiled but 16 sanitizer test links (`tests/engine_library/host_*_tsan`, `audio_sample_tsan` and others) failed with `errno=28`, the disk being full; both arm64 trees were then deleted for space (four macOS trees take about 17 GB of the Mac's 113 GB volume; 11 GB were free afterwards). Nothing ran on arm64, including the JIT (the Apple silicon finding for the future Android and iOS port is in the day file: `LUAJIT_ENABLE_OSX_HRT` and the allow-jit entitlement for a hardened app, the interpreter on iOS). (4) Hashes: `script_sample` `e3e3ca13a45686f9` equals Linux's; `script_demo --frames 120` ends on `e1f5c33055210a41` on the Mac (1, 2 and 8 threads), on the PC (Day 473, 1 to 16 threads) and on Linux (recorded 2026-11-19, 1, 2, 8 and 16 threads at `ce90ed09`): equal on the three machines. (5) The `HashMap` of `eefa8c3b` was built on Linux by the Container audit's Day 1 and replaced since by the series' `OpenTable` version (Days 2-7), which the watcher was rebuilt against on the Mac when this day was rebased onto it. (6) The Windows branch of the root `CMakeLists.txt` lacked `engine/script` before `tools/asset_mint`: Day 473 adds the script directories there. (7) FSEvents limits: a rename inside the directory is reported as `Removed` and `Created`, never `Renamed`; a write that keeps the size and mtime and arrives in the same latency window as a metadata-only change of that name may be taken as metadata only and not reported; events are coalesced over 50 ms. (8) The regression test `a name that was there before the watch and goes at once is reported removed (Day 474 review)` passes with and without its fix, so it does not prove it.
- **Phase 27 Day 465 (2026-11-18, Linux):** debugging, with these limits: (1) a `pause` command and a new breakpoint apply to calls that start afterwards: the backend asks `DebugSession::WantsLines` at the start of each call and after each stop, so a callback already running (a long loop) is not interrupted. (2) A step that ends with its callback (a `step_over` at the last line, a `step_out`) stays armed: the next call of that partition stops at its first line with reason `step`, and the host's own frames (`behavior_host`) show up in a `step_out` stop. (3) Only the partitions' states are debuggable: the task pool's states (Day 461) and the scratch states of the reloader and the console have no session. (4) With `pause_all`, a partition waits at the gate only between calls (a callback that is already running when another partition stops finishes first, and a callback that starts at the same moment may still run); a console chunk waits at the gate too, and a breakpoint can stop a console chunk or a script's top level while it loads. (5) The profiler times calls and returns in the hook (the cost is a hook call per Lua function entry and exit, not measured): a coroutine that yields leaves its frames on the profile stack until the next call clears it, so a function that yielded adds no time; a tail call is not timed separately. (6) The hot-script warning of Day 458 is still not built (declined there); the profile does not feed one. (7) `ConsoleRun` on a partition needs the partition free (between ticks); `ConsoleSubmit` runs at the start of a tick and does not run while ticks are not made. A console chunk sees the partition's globals but not an entity's `self`. (8) The repeat count of an error is kept in `RecentErrors()` for the last 64 records only; a record pushed out of the list stops counting, though the log, the stats and the events still do. (9) The network adapter for an external debugger is not in this phase. (10) Built and tested on Windows on Day 473 (MSVC and clang-cl, unity on and off, the shared library) and on the Mac on Day 474 (statically linked only).
- **Phase 27 Day 466 (2026-11-18, Linux):** physics bindings, with these limits: (1) `physics.set_gravity_scale` changes the `rigid_body` component at the merge, so a `component.get` in the same tick still shows the old scale. (2) A force, velocity or wake on an entity whose body does not exist yet (created this tick) is an error at the call: the body exists after the next sync. (3) Physics calls from the console and from task states see no physics (those have no world context). (4) The mesh/hull/compound colliders are set by asset id in `collider`; the day's tests cover primitives only. (5) `Nested<>` in `script_physics/physics_components.cpp` could live in `storage` if another module needs it. (6) Observed: a GCC unity build of `engine/net` fails with `-Werror=subobject-linkage` (`net/async.cpp`, not from this day; `script_runtime.cpp` had the same failure at HEAD and is fixed). (7) Built and tested on Windows on Day 473 (MSVC and clang-cl, unity on and off, the shared library) and on the Mac on Day 474 (statically linked only).
- **Phase 27 Day 467 (2026-11-19, Linux):** physics events and characters, with these limits: (1) an event is built only for an entity with a script listener, so a native `EventBus` subscriber of `on_collision_*` or `on_trigger_*` sees nothing for entities with no script. (2) Two scripts that call `character.create` for one entity in one tick both pass the call-time check and the merge keeps the first; the second gets no error. (3) A character's body names no entity (`SyncBodies` destroys a body that names an entity without a `RigidBody`), so `EmitPhysicsEvents` needs `PhysicsEventOptions::characters` to name it, and another module reading `PhysicsEvents` sees an invalid entity for it. (4) The character moves at fixed steps but its `LocalTransform` is written once per frame (`CharacterSet::WriteBack`, no interpolation). (5) A trigger event goes to the trigger's entity only, not to the body that entered. (6) The measurement "100,000 events cost nothing for entities with no handler" was not taken (the test checks that nothing reaches the bus). (7) Raycast vehicles and ragdolls are library scripts of the demo (Day 475), not built. (8) Built and tested on Windows on Day 473 (MSVC and clang-cl, unity on and off, the shared library) and on the Mac on Day 474 (statically linked only).
- **Scripts set the sky and the lights (2026-11-22, Linux):** not built on Windows or macOS (`engine/engine_c/script_scene.cpp`, `tests/engine_library/host_script_scene.c` and its CMake lines). The picture part of `host_script_scene` runs only where an off-screen Vulkan device can be made and is left out of the tsan flavor (the GPU driver's threads); under asan it runs with `detect_leaks=0` like the other off-screen tests. `test_phase_script_long_warning` failed once in a parallel plain run and passed alone.
- **A display that glows and reflects (2026-11-22, Linux):** `shaders/gbuffer.frag.slang` and `lighting.frag.slang` changed and only the SPIR-V outputs were minted on Linux. **The PC re-minted the DXIL of both** (2026-11-22, `slangc v2026.18.2`, `dxcompiler 1.9.2602.17`; no other `.dxil.inc` changed): `build-msvc` builds, the `shaders` label passes (25 of 25, `shaders_check` included), the DX12 pipeline and emissive tests pass (16 of 16) and `engine_c_glass_screen` passes (plain MSVC; asan and clang-cl not run). The Metal libraries were re-minted on the Mac (`d838e678`); what `shaders_check` says there was not recorded here. `engine_c_glass_screen` is a skipped stub under tsan (the GPU driver) and runs with `detect_leaks=0` under asan.
